  s/\t  s/\t         s/\t     s/\t
 {} {3}{2}{2,3}   {1}{1,3} {1,2}{1,2,3}

The 8 leaves are exactly the 2³ subsets. Each edge is a choose; each return is an un-choose that pops the last element so the buffer is reused.

Pruning ideas: sort first and skip duplicates (if(i>start && a[i]==a[i-1]) continue;) for unique subsets; cut a branch when a partial sum already exceeds the target; bound the remaining best-possible against the current best.

4 · Divide & Conquer / Tree DP

Flowchart leaf: count / generate many trees

The idea

Split a problem at a pivot into independent sub-problems, solve each recursively, then combine. "Count/generate all trees" is the poster child: fix each value as the root, recursively build all left subtrees and all right subtrees, and take the Cartesian product. Memoize sub-answers when ranges repeat.

Brute force — build then validate Catalan · n

Generating every labelling and checking the BST property afterwards revisits identical sub-structures over and over. D&C builds only valid shapes and (with memoization) reuses shared sub-ranges.

Generate all unique BSTs

List<TreeNode> generateTrees(int n){ return build(1, n); }
List<TreeNode> build(int lo, int hi){
    List<TreeNode> res = new ArrayList<>();
    if (lo > hi){ res.add(null); return res; }          // one empty shape
    for (int root = lo; root <= hi; root++)
        for (TreeNode l : build(lo, root-1))            // all left subtrees
            for (TreeNode r : build(root+1, hi)){       // all right subtrees
                TreeNode node = new TreeNode(root);
                node.left = l; node.right = r;
                res.add(node);
            }
    return res;
}
std::vector<TreeNode*> generateTrees(int n){ return build(1, n); }
std::vector<TreeNode*> build(int lo, int hi){
    std::vector<TreeNode*> res;
    if (lo > hi){ res.push_back(nullptr); return res; }          // one empty shape
    for (int root = lo; root <= hi; root++)
        for (TreeNode* l : build(lo, root-1))            // all left subtrees
            for (TreeNode* r : build(root+1, hi)){       // all right subtrees
                TreeNode* node = new TreeNode(root);
                node->left = l; node->right = r;
                res.push_back(node);
            }
    return res;
}
def generate_trees(n):
    return build(1, n)

def build(lo, hi):
    res = []
    if lo > hi:
        res.append(None)          # one empty shape
        return res
    for root in range(lo, hi+1):
        for l in build(lo, root-1):            # all left subtrees
            for r in build(root+1, hi):       # all right subtrees
                node = TreeNode(root)
                node.left = l
                node.right = r
                res.append(node)
    return res
function generateTrees(n: number): (TreeNode | null)[] { return build(1, n); }
function build(lo: number, hi: number): (TreeNode | null)[] {
    const res: (TreeNode | null)[] = [];
    if (lo > hi) { res.push(null); return res; }          // one empty shape
    for (let root = lo; root <= hi; root++)
        for (const l of build(lo, root-1))            // all left subtrees
            for (const r of build(root+1, hi)) {       // all right subtrees
                const node = new TreeNode(root);
                node.left = l; node.right = r;
                res.push(node);
            }
    return res;
}

Just the count (Catalan via DP)

int numTrees(int n){
    int[] dp = new int[n+1];
    dp[0] = dp[1] = 1;
    for (int nodes=2; nodes<=n; nodes++)
        for (int root=1; root<=nodes; root++)
            dp[nodes] += dp[root-1] * dp[nodes-root];   // left size × right size
    return dp[n];
}
int numTrees(int n){
    std::vector<int> dp(n+1, 0);
    dp[0] = dp[1] = 1;
    for (int nodes=2; nodes<=n; nodes++)
        for (int root=1; root<=nodes; root++)
            dp[nodes] += dp[root-1] * dp[nodes-root];   // left size × right size
    return dp[n];
}
def num_trees(n):
    dp = [0] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = dp[1] = 1
    for nodes in range(2, n + 1):
        for root in range(1, nodes + 1):
            dp[nodes] += dp[root-1] * dp[nodes-root]   # left size × right size
    return dp[n]
function numTrees(n: number): number {
    const dp = new Array<number>(n+1).fill(0);
    dp[0] = dp[1] = 1;
    for (let nodes = 2; nodes <= n; nodes++)
        for (let root = 1; root <= nodes; root++)
            dp[nodes] += dp[root-1] * dp[nodes-root];   // left size × right size
    return dp[n];
}

Worked example — n = 3

RootLeft rangeRight range# shapes
1∅ (1 shape){2,3} (2 shapes)1×2 = 2
2{1} (1){3} (1)1×1 = 1
3{1,2} (2)∅ (1)2×1 = 2

Total = 2+1+2 = 5 = Catalan(3). The DP formula dp[3]=dp[0]dp[2]+dp[1]dp[1]+dp[2]dp[0] gives the same 5 in O(n²).

5 · Topological Sort

Flowchart leaf: DAG-related

The idea

Order the vertices of a directed acyclic graph so every edge points forward — the classic "which order can I take these courses / build these targets" question. Two standard methods: Kahn's (BFS on in-degrees) and DFS post-order reversed. Kahn's also detects cycles for free: if you emit fewer than n nodes, a cycle blocked the rest.

Kahn's algorithm, step by step

  1. Compute in-degree (number of incoming edges) for every node.
  2. Queue all nodes with in-degree 0 — they depend on nothing.
  3. Pop one, append to the order, and decrement each successor's in-degree; if a successor hits 0, queue it.
  4. When the queue empties, if you emitted every node you have a valid order; otherwise the graph has a cycle.

Brute force — repeatedly scan for a free node O(V²)

Each round, linearly search for any node whose prerequisites are all done, output it, mark it done, repeat. Correct but quadratic; the in-degree queue makes it linear.

Optimal — Kahn's O(V + E)

int[] topoSort(int n, int[][] edges){
    List<Integer>[] adj = new List[n];
    for (int i=0;i<n;i++) adj[i]=new ArrayList<>();
    int[] indeg = new int[n];
    for (int[] e : edges){ adj[e[0]].add(e[1]); indeg[e[1]]++; }

    Queue<Integer> q = new ArrayDeque<>();
    for (int i=0;i<n;i++) if (indeg[i]==0) q.offer(i);

    int[] order = new int[n]; int k=0;
    while (!q.isEmpty()){
        int u = q.poll(); order[k++] = u;
        for (int v : adj[u]) if (--indeg[v]==0) q.offer(v);
    }
    return k==n ? order : new int[0];        // empty ⇒ cycle
}
vector<int> topoSort(int n, vector<vector<int>>& edges){
    vector<vector<int>> adj(n);
    vector<int> indeg(n, 0);
    for (auto& e : edges){ adj[e[0]].push_back(e[1]); indeg[e[1]]++; }

    queue<int> q;
    for (int i=0;i<n;i++) if (indeg[i]==0) q.push(i);

    vector<int> order(n); int k=0;
    while (!q.empty()){
        int u = q.front(); q.pop(); order[k++] = u;
        for (int v : adj[u]) if (--indeg[v]==0) q.push(v);
    }
    return k==n ? order : vector<int>();        // empty ⇒ cycle
}
def topo_sort(n, edges):
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    indeg = [0] * n
    for e in edges:
        adj[e[0]].append(e[1])
        indeg[e[1]] += 1

    q = deque()
    for i in range(n):
        if indeg[i] == 0:
            q.append(i)

    order = [0] * n
    k = 0
    while q:
        u = q.popleft()
        order[k] = u
        k += 1
        for v in adj[u]:
            indeg[v] -= 1
            if indeg[v] == 0:
                q.append(v)
    return order if k == n else []        # empty ⇒ cycle
function topoSort(n: number, edges: number[][]): number[] {
    const adj: number[][] = Array.from({ length: n }, () => []);
    const indeg = new Array(n).fill(0);
    for (const e of edges) { adj[e[0]].push(e[1]); indeg[e[1]]++; }

    const q: number[] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) if (indeg[i] === 0) q.push(i);

    const order = new Array(n).fill(0);
    let k = 0;
    let head = 0;
    while (head < q.length) {
        const u = q[head++]; order[k++] = u;
        for (const v of adj[u]) if (--indeg[v] === 0) q.push(v);
    }
    return k === n ? order : [];        // empty ⇒ cycle
}

Worked example — 4 courses

Edges (prereq → course): 0→1, 0→2, 1→3, 2→3.

StepIn-degreesQueueEmit
init[0,1,1,2][0]—
pop 0[-,0,0,2][1,2]0
pop 1[-,-,0,1][2]0,1
pop 2[-,-,-,0][3]0,1,2
pop 3—[]0,1,2,3

Order [0,1,2,3]; 4 of 4 emitted ⇒ no cycle. ([0,2,1,3] is equally valid.)

Pitfall: to detect an impossible schedule you must check the emitted count, not assume success — a cycle silently leaves nodes stuck at in-degree > 0.

6 · Dijkstra & the Shortest-Path Family

Flowchart leaf: weighted shortest path

The idea

Shortest paths from a source in a graph with non-negative weights. Dijkstra greedily settles the closest un-settled node using a min-heap. The invariant: when a node is popped for the first time, its shortest distance is final, because any other route would have to pass through a farther (hence not-yet-popped) node.

Step by step

  1. dist[src]=0, everything else ∞; push (src,0).
  2. Pop the smallest-distance entry. If it's stale (d > dist[u]) skip it.
  3. Relax each edge u→v: if dist[u]+w < dist[v], improve dist[v] and push the new pair.
  4. Repeat until the heap empties.

Brute force — Bellman-Ford O(V·E)

Relax all edges V−1 times. Slower, but it is the correct choice when weights can be negative (Dijkstra breaks on negative edges), and it detects negative cycles.

for (int i=0;i<n-1;i++)
    for (int[] e : edges)                // e = {u, v, w}
        if (dist[e[0]]+e[2] < dist[e[1]]) dist[e[1]] = dist[e[0]]+e[2];
for (int i=0;i<n-1;i++)
    for (auto& e : edges)                // e = {u, v, w}
        if (dist[e[0]]+e[2] < dist[e[1]]) dist[e[1]] = dist[e[0]]+e[2];
for i in range(n - 1):
    for e in edges:                      # e = [u, v, w]
        if dist[e[0]] + e[2] < dist[e[1]]:
            dist[e[1]] = dist[e[0]] + e[2]
for (let i = 0; i < n - 1; i++)
    for (const e of edges)               // e = [u, v, w]
        if (dist[e[0]] + e[2] < dist[e[1]]) dist[e[1]] = dist[e[0]] + e[2];

Optimal — Dijkstra with a binary heap O(E log V)

int[] dijkstra(List<int[]>[] adj, int src, int n){   // adj[u] = {v, w}
    int[] dist = new int[n];
    Arrays.fill(dist, Integer.MAX_VALUE);
    dist[src] = 0;
    PriorityQueue<int[]> pq = new PriorityQueue<>((a,b)->a[1]-b[1]);
    pq.offer(new int[]{src,0});
    while (!pq.isEmpty()){
        int[] top = pq.poll();
        int u = top[0], d = top[1];
        if (d > dist[u]) continue;                    // stale entry, skip
        for (int[] e : adj[u]){
            int v = e[0], w = e[1];
            if (dist[u]+w < dist[v]){
                dist[v] = dist[u]+w;
                pq.offer(new int[]{v, dist[v]});
            }
        }
    }
    return dist;
}
vector<int> dijkstra(vector<vector<pair<int,int>>>& adj, int src, int n){   // adj[u] = {v, w}
    vector<int> dist(n, INT_MAX);
    dist[src] = 0;
    auto cmp = [](const pair<int,int>& a, const pair<int,int>& b){ return a.second > b.second; };
    priority_queue<pair<int,int>, vector<pair<int,int>>, decltype(cmp)> pq(cmp);
    pq.push({src, 0});
    while (!pq.empty()){
        auto top = pq.top(); pq.pop();
        int u = top.first, d = top.second;
        if (d > dist[u]) continue;                    // stale entry, skip
        for (auto& e : adj[u]){
            int v = e.first, w = e.second;
            if (dist[u]+w < dist[v]){
                dist[v] = dist[u]+w;
                pq.push({v, dist[v]});
            }
        }
    }
    return dist;
}
def dijkstra(adj, src, n):   # adj[u] = [(v, w), ...]
    dist = [float('inf')] * n
    dist[src] = 0
    pq = [(0, src)]            # (d, u)
    while pq:
        d, u = heapq.heappop(pq)
        if d > dist[u]:
            continue                              # stale entry, skip
        for v, w in adj[u]:
            if dist[u] + w < dist[v]:
                dist[v] = dist[u] + w
                heapq.heappush(pq, (dist[v], v))
    return dist
function dijkstra(adj: [number, number][][], src: number, n: number): number[] {   // adj[u] = [v, w]
    const dist = new Array(n).fill(Infinity);
    dist[src] = 0;
    const pq: [number, number][] = [[src, 0]];   // [u, d]; array used as a simple priority queue
    while (pq.length) {
        pq.sort((a, b) => a[1] - b[1]);
        const [u, d] = pq.shift()!;
        if (d > dist[u]) continue;                    // stale entry, skip
        for (const [v, w] of adj[u]) {
            if (dist[u] + w < dist[v]) {
                dist[v] = dist[u] + w;
                pq.push([v, dist[v]]);
            }
        }
    }
    return dist;
}

Worked example, traced

Edges: 0→1(4), 0→2(1), 2→1(2), 1→3(1), 2→3(5). Source 0.

Pop (node,d)Relaxationsdist after
(0,0)1←4, 2←1[0,4,1,∞]
(2,1)1←1+2=3 (better!), 3←1+5=6[0,3,1,6]
(1,3)3←3+1=4 (better!)[0,3,1,4]
(1,4) stale4>dist[1]=3 → skip[0,3,1,4]
(3,4)no outgoing improvements[0,3,1,4]

Final distances [0,3,1,4]. The stale (1,4) entry shows why the d > dist[u] guard matters — it silently discards outdated heap entries instead of re-processing them.

Family map: all-same-weight → BFS (§1); weights ∈ {0,1} → 0-1 BFS deque; non-negative weights → Dijkstra; negative edges → Bellman-Ford; all-pairs → Floyd-Warshall O(V³).

7 · Union-Find (Disjoint Set Union)

Flowchart leaf: connectivity

The idea

Maintain a partition of n elements into disjoint sets with two near-O(1) operations: find(x) (which set?) and union(a,b) (merge two sets). Perfect for "are these connected", "count components", Kruskal's MST, and detecting cycles as you add edges. Two optimizations make it almost constant: path compression (flatten the tree during find) and union by rank/size (attach the smaller tree under the larger).

Brute force — BFS/DFS per query O(query · (V+E))

Answering each "connected?" by a fresh graph search is fine for a few queries but collapses under many interleaved unions and queries — exactly where DSU shines with near-constant amortized cost.

Optimal — DSU with both optimizations ~O(α(n)) per op

class DSU {
    int[] parent, rank; int count;
    DSU(int n){
        parent = new int[n]; rank = new int[n]; count = n;
        for (int i=0;i<n;i++) parent[i]=i;
    }
    int find(int x){
        while (parent[x]!=x){ parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]; x=parent[x]; } // compress
        return x;
    }
    boolean union(int a,int b){
        int ra=find(a), rb=find(b);
        if (ra==rb) return false;               // already same set (edge forms a cycle)
        if (rank[ra]<rank[rb]){ int t=ra; ra=rb; rb=t; }
        parent[rb]=ra;                          // smaller under larger
        if (rank[ra]==rank[rb]) rank[ra]++;
        count--;                                // one fewer component
        return true;
    }
}
class DSU {
public:
    vector<int> parent, rank; int count;
    DSU(int n){
        parent.resize(n); rank.assign(n, 0); count = n;
        for (int i=0;i<n;i++) parent[i]=i;
    }
    int find(int x){
        while (parent[x]!=x){ parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]; x=parent[x]; } // compress
        return x;
    }
    bool unite(int a,int b){                // 'union' is a reserved keyword in C++
        int ra=find(a), rb=find(b);
        if (ra==rb) return false;               // already same set (edge forms a cycle)
        if (rank[ra]<rank[rb]){ int t=ra; ra=rb; rb=t; }
        parent[rb]=ra;                          // smaller under larger
        if (rank[ra]==rank[rb]) rank[ra]++;
        count--;                                // one fewer component
        return true;
    }
};
class DSU:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.parent = list(range(n))
        self.rank = [0] * n
        self.count = n

    def find(self, x):
        while self.parent[x] != x:
            self.parent[x] = self.parent[self.parent[x]]
            x = self.parent[x]                  # compress
        return x

    def union(self, a, b):
        ra, rb = self.find(a), self.find(b)
        if ra == rb:
            return False                         # already same set (edge forms a cycle)
        if self.rank[ra] < self.rank[rb]:
            ra, rb = rb, ra
        self.parent[rb] = ra                     # smaller under larger
        if self.rank[ra] == self.rank[rb]:
            self.rank[ra] += 1
        self.count -= 1                          # one fewer component
        return True
class DSU {
    parent: number[];
    rank: number[];
    count: number;

    constructor(n: number) {
        this.parent = Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => i);
        this.rank = new Array(n).fill(0);
        this.count = n;
    }

    find(x: number): number {
        while (this.parent[x] !== x) {
            this.parent[x] = this.parent[this.parent[x]];
            x = this.parent[x];                  // compress
        }
        return x;
    }

    union(a: number, b: number): boolean {
        let ra = this.find(a), rb = this.find(b);
        if (ra === rb) return false;              // already same set (edge forms a cycle)
        if (this.rank[ra] < this.rank[rb]) { [ra, rb] = [rb, ra]; }
        this.parent[rb] = ra;                     // smaller under larger
        if (this.rank[ra] === this.rank[rb]) this.rank[ra]++;
        this.count--;                             // one fewer component
        return true;
    }
}

Worked example — number of provinces

n=5. Process edges (0,1), (1,2), (3,4):

Unionparent[] (roots)count
start0 1 2 3 45
(0,1)0 0 2 3 44
(1,2)0 0 0 3 43
(3,4)0 0 0 3 32

find(0)==find(2) → true (both root 0). Components = 2: {0,1,2} and {3,4}. Trying union(0,2) returns false (same set) — that "false" is how Kruskal skips cycle-forming edges.

α(n) is the inverse Ackermann function — ≤ 4 for any n you'll ever see, so treat DSU ops as effectively constant.

8 · Binary Search — on an Array and on the Answer

Flowchart leaves: binary search (sorted) binary search on the answer (max/min)

The idea

If the search space is monotonic — sorted values, or a yes/no predicate that flips once from false to true — you can halve it each step. Two flavours: searching a sorted array for a value/boundary, and searching the answer range for the smallest/largest value that satisfies a feasibility test. The second is the interview power move: "minimize the max…" / "maximize the min…" almost always means binary-search-the-answer.

Brute force — linear scan / try every candidate O(n) or O(range)

Walk the array element by element, or try every possible answer value and test feasibility. Correct but ignores the monotonic structure that lets you skip half the space each comparison.

Template A · exact / boundary search in a sorted array O(log n)

int lowerBound(int[] a, int target){       // first index with a[i] >= target
    int lo = 0, hi = a.length;              // [lo, hi) half-open
    while (lo < hi){
        int mid = lo + (hi - lo)/2;
        if (a[mid] < target) lo = mid + 1;
        else hi = mid;
    }
    return lo;                              // insertion point; == length if none
}
int lowerBound(vector<int>& a, int target){       // first index with a[i] >= target
    int lo = 0, hi = a.size();              // [lo, hi) half-open
    while (lo < hi){
        int mid = lo + (hi - lo)/2;
        if (a[mid] < target) lo = mid + 1;
        else hi = mid;
    }
    return lo;                              // insertion point; == length if none
}
def lower_bound(a, target):       # first index with a[i] >= target
    lo, hi = 0, len(a)            # [lo, hi) half-open
    while lo < hi:
        mid = lo + (hi - lo) // 2
        if a[mid] < target:
            lo = mid + 1
        else:
            hi = mid
    return lo                     # insertion point; == length if none
function lowerBound(a: number[], target: number): number {       // first index with a[i] >= target
    let lo = 0, hi = a.length;              // [lo, hi) half-open
    while (lo < hi) {
        const mid = lo + Math.floor((hi - lo) / 2);
        if (a[mid] < target) lo = mid + 1;
        else hi = mid;
    }
    return lo;                              // insertion point; == length if none
}

Template B · binary search on the answer (feasibility predicate)

// smallest speed so Koko finishes all piles within h hours
int minEatingSpeed(int[] piles, int h){
    int lo = 1, hi = 0;
    for (int p : piles) hi = Math.max(hi, p);
    while (lo < hi){
        int mid = lo + (hi - lo)/2;
        if (feasible(piles, mid, h)) hi = mid;   // can do it ⇒ try slower
        else lo = mid + 1;                       // too slow ⇒ speed up
    }
    return lo;                                   // smallest feasible speed
}
boolean feasible(int[] piles, int speed, int h){
    long hours = 0;
    for (int p : piles) hours += (p + speed - 1) / speed;  // ceil
    return hours <= h;
}
// smallest speed so Koko finishes all piles within h hours
int minEatingSpeed(vector<int>& piles, int h){
    int lo = 1, hi = 0;
    for (int p : piles) hi = max(hi, p);
    while (lo < hi){
        int mid = lo + (hi - lo)/2;
        if (feasible(piles, mid, h)) hi = mid;   // can do it ⇒ try slower
        else lo = mid + 1;                       // too slow ⇒ speed up
    }
    return lo;                                   // smallest feasible speed
}
bool feasible(vector<int>& piles, int speed, int h){
    long long hours = 0;
    for (int p : piles) hours += (p + speed - 1) / speed;  // ceil
    return hours <= h;
}
def min_eating_speed(piles, h):
    # smallest speed so Koko finishes all piles within h hours
    lo, hi = 1, max(piles)
    while lo < hi:
        mid = lo + (hi - lo) // 2
        if feasible(piles, mid, h):
            hi = mid                              # can do it ⇒ try slower
        else:
            lo = mid + 1                          # too slow ⇒ speed up
    return lo                                     # smallest feasible speed

def feasible(piles, speed, h):
    hours = 0
    for p in piles:
        hours += (p + speed - 1) // speed         # ceil
    return hours <= h
// smallest speed so Koko finishes all piles within h hours
function minEatingSpeed(piles: number[], h: number): number {
    let lo = 1, hi = 0;
    for (const p of piles) hi = Math.max(hi, p);
    while (lo < hi) {
        const mid = lo + Math.floor((hi - lo) / 2);
        if (feasible(piles, mid, h)) hi = mid;   // can do it ⇒ try slower
        else lo = mid + 1;                       // too slow ⇒ speed up
    }
    return lo;                                   // smallest feasible speed
}
function feasible(piles: number[], speed: number, h: number): boolean {
    let hours = 0;
    for (const p of piles) hours += Math.ceil(p / speed);   // ceil
    return hours <= h;
}

Worked example — binary search on the answer, traced

piles=[3,6,7,11], h=8. Feasible speeds form the monotonic pattern F F F T T …; we want the first T. Range [1,11]:

lohimidhours at midfeasible?move
11161+1+2+2=6yeshi=6
1631+2+3+4=10nolo=4
4651+2+2+3=8yeshi=5
4541+2+2+3=8yeshi=4
44lo==hi → answer 4

Answer 4. Note we never enumerate speeds — 4 probes cover the whole range 1..11.

Pitfalls: off-by-one is the whole game. Pin down whether your interval is [lo,hi] (loop while lo<=hi) or [lo,hi) (loop while lo<hi), and use lo+(hi-lo)/2 to dodge integer overflow. For "largest feasible", mirror the moves.

9 · Heap / Priority Queue

Flowchart leaves: kth smallest/largest merge k sorted lists

The idea

A binary heap gives O(log n) insert and O(log n) extract-min/max with O(1) peek. Reach for it whenever you repeatedly need "the current best/worst among a changing set": top-k, kth element, streaming medians, merging sorted sequences, or always expanding the cheapest option (Dijkstra, Prim).

Brute force — sort everything O(n log n)

Sort the whole array and index the kth element. Fine once, but wasteful for streams or when k ≪ n; a size-k heap does it in O(n log k) and handles data that doesn't fit in memory.

Template A · kth largest with a size-k min-heap O(n log k)

int findKthLargest(int[] nums, int k){
    PriorityQueue<Integer> min = new PriorityQueue<>();  // keeps k largest
    for (int x : nums){
        min.offer(x);
        if (min.size() > k) min.poll();     // evict the smallest
    }
    return min.peek();                       // kth largest overall
}
int findKthLargest(vector<int>& nums, int k){
    priority_queue<int, vector<int>, greater<int>> minHeap;  // keeps k largest
    for (int x : nums){
        minHeap.push(x);
        if (minHeap.size() > (size_t)k) minHeap.pop();     // evict the smallest
    }
    return minHeap.top();                       // kth largest overall
}
def find_kth_largest(nums, k):
    heap = []                                # keeps k largest (min-heap)
    for x in nums:
        heapq.heappush(heap, x)
        if len(heap) > k:
            heapq.heappop(heap)              # evict the smallest
    return heap[0]                           # kth largest overall
function findKthLargest(nums: number[], k: number): number {
    const min: number[] = [];               // keeps k largest
    for (const x of nums) {
        min.push(x);
        min.sort((a, b) => a - b);
        if (min.length > k) min.shift();     // evict the smallest
    }
    return min[0];                           // kth largest overall
}

Template B · merge k sorted lists O(N log k)

ListNode mergeKLists(ListNode[] lists){
    PriorityQueue<ListNode> pq = new PriorityQueue<>((a,b)->a.val-b.val);
    for (ListNode l : lists) if (l!=null) pq.offer(l);
    ListNode dummy = new ListNode(0), tail = dummy;
    while (!pq.isEmpty()){
        ListNode n = pq.poll();
        tail.next = n; tail = n;
        if (n.next != null) pq.offer(n.next);
    }
    return dummy.next;
}
ListNode* mergeKLists(vector<ListNode*>& lists){
    auto cmp = [](ListNode* a, ListNode* b){ return a->val > b->val; };
    priority_queue<ListNode*, vector<ListNode*>, decltype(cmp)> pq(cmp);
    for (ListNode* l : lists) if (l != nullptr) pq.push(l);
    ListNode dummy(0), *tail = &dummy;
    while (!pq.empty()){
        ListNode* n = pq.top(); pq.pop();
        tail->next = n; tail = n;
        if (n->next != nullptr) pq.push(n->next);
    }
    return dummy.next;
}
def merge_k_lists(lists):
    heap = []
    for i, l in enumerate(lists):
        if l is not None:
            heapq.heappush(heap, (l.val, i, l))     # tie-break with index (nodes aren't comparable)
    dummy = ListNode(0)
    tail = dummy
    counter = len(heap)
    while heap:
        val, idx, n = heapq.heappop(heap)
        tail.next = n
        tail = n
        if n.next is not None:
            counter += 1
            heapq.heappush(heap, (n.next.val, counter, n.next))
    return dummy.next
function mergeKLists(lists: (ListNode | null)[]): ListNode | null {
    const pq: ListNode[] = [];
    for (const l of lists) if (l !== null) pq.push(l);
    pq.sort((a, b) => a.val - b.val);
    const dummy = new ListNode(0);
    let tail = dummy;
    while (pq.length) {
        const n = pq.shift()!;
        tail.next = n; tail = n;
        if (n.next !== null) {
            pq.push(n.next);
            pq.sort((a, b) => a.val - b.val);
        }
    }
    return dummy.next;
}

Template C · two heaps for a running median

PriorityQueue<Integer> lo = new PriorityQueue<>(Collections.reverseOrder()); // max-heap, lower half
PriorityQueue<Integer> hi = new PriorityQueue<>();                          // min-heap, upper half
void add(int x){
    lo.offer(x); hi.offer(lo.poll());              // funnel through to keep order
    if (hi.size() > lo.size()) lo.offer(hi.poll()); // rebalance sizes
}
double median(){ return lo.size()>hi.size() ? lo.peek() : (lo.peek()+hi.peek())/2.0; }
priority_queue<int> lo;                              // max-heap, lower half
priority_queue<int, vector<int>, greater<int>> hi;   // min-heap, upper half
void add(int x){
    lo.push(x); hi.push(lo.top()); lo.pop();          // funnel through to keep order
    if (hi.size() > lo.size()){ lo.push(hi.top()); hi.pop(); } // rebalance sizes
}
double median(){ return lo.size()>hi.size() ? lo.top() : (lo.top()+hi.top())/2.0; }
lo = []   # max-heap, lower half (store negated values)
hi = []   # min-heap, upper half

def add(x):
    heapq.heappush(lo, -x)
    heapq.heappush(hi, -heapq.heappop(lo))            # funnel through to keep order
    if len(hi) > len(lo):
        heapq.heappush(lo, -heapq.heappop(hi))        # rebalance sizes

def median():
    if len(lo) > len(hi):
        return -lo[0]
    return (-lo[0] + hi[0]) / 2.0
const lo: number[] = [];   // max-heap, lower half
const hi: number[] = [];   // min-heap, upper half

function add(x: number): void {
    lo.push(x); lo.sort((a, b) => b - a);
    hi.push(lo.shift()!); hi.sort((a, b) => a - b);   // funnel through to keep order
    if (hi.length > lo.length) {
        lo.push(hi.shift()!);
        lo.sort((a, b) => b - a);                      // rebalance sizes
    }
}

function median(): number {
    return lo.length > hi.length ? lo[0] : (lo[0] + hi[0]) / 2.0;
}

Worked example — 2nd largest of [3,2,1,5,6,4], k=2

ReadHeap (min on top)Action
3[3]size 1
2[2,3]size 2
1[2,3]add 1 → [1,2,3], size 3 > 2 → poll 1
5[3,5]add 5 → poll 2
6[5,6]add 6 → poll 3
4[5,6]add 4 → poll 4

Heap holds the two largest {5,6}; its min (top) = 5 is the 2nd largest.

10 · Two Pointers

Flowchart leaves: linked-list fast/slow array partitioning two-sequence merge find indices O(1) memory

The idea

Two indices move through the data with a rule, replacing a nested loop with a single linear pass and O(1) memory. Three sub-patterns: opposite ends converging (sorted two-sum, container with most water, palindrome), fast/slow on a linked list (cycle, middle, nth-from-end), and one pointer per sequence (merge, count pairs across two sorted arrays).

Brute force — nested loops O(n²)

Check every pair (i,j). The monotonic structure (usually "the array is sorted") means when a sum is too big you never need a larger j — so one pointer only ever moves inward, collapsing the double loop.

Template A · opposite ends — two-sum on a sorted array O(n)

int[] twoSum(int[] a, int target){        // a sorted ascending
    int lo = 0, hi = a.length - 1;
    while (lo < hi){
        int sum = a[lo] + a[hi];
        if (sum == target) return new int[]{lo, hi};
        else if (sum < target) lo++;      // need larger → move left pointer up
        else hi--;                        // need smaller → move right pointer down
    }
    return new int[]{-1,-1};
}
vector<int> twoSum(vector<int>& a, int target){        // a sorted ascending
    int lo = 0, hi = a.size() - 1;
    while (lo < hi){
        int sum = a[lo] + a[hi];
        if (sum == target) return {lo, hi};
        else if (sum < target) lo++;      // need larger → move left pointer up
        else hi--;                        // need smaller → move right pointer down
    }
    return {-1, -1};
}
def two_sum(a, target):        # a sorted ascending
    lo, hi = 0, len(a) - 1
    while lo < hi:
        s = a[lo] + a[hi]
        if s == target:
            return [lo, hi]
        elif s < target:
            lo += 1             # need larger → move left pointer up
        else:
            hi -= 1             # need smaller → move right pointer down
    return [-1, -1]
function twoSum(a: number[], target: number): number[] {        // a sorted ascending
    let lo = 0, hi = a.length - 1;
    while (lo < hi) {
        const sum = a[lo] + a[hi];
        if (sum === target) return [lo, hi];
        else if (sum < target) lo++;      // need larger → move left pointer up
        else hi--;                        // need smaller → move right pointer down
    }
    return [-1, -1];
}

Template B · fast/slow — cycle detection & middle

boolean hasCycle(ListNode head){
    ListNode slow = head, fast = head;
    while (fast != null && fast.next != null){
        slow = slow.next; fast = fast.next.next;   // 1x vs 2x speed
        if (slow == fast) return true;             // they meet inside a loop
    }
    return false;
}
bool hasCycle(ListNode* head){
    ListNode *slow = head, *fast = head;
    while (fast != nullptr && fast->next != nullptr){
        slow = slow->next; fast = fast->next->next;   // 1x vs 2x speed
        if (slow == fast) return true;                // they meet inside a loop
    }
    return false;
}
def has_cycle(head):
    slow, fast = head, head
    while fast is not None and fast.next is not None:
        slow = slow.next
        fast = fast.next.next                # 1x vs 2x speed
        if slow == fast:
            return True                       # they meet inside a loop
    return False
function hasCycle(head: ListNode | null): boolean {
    let slow = head, fast = head;
    while (fast !== null && fast.next !== null) {
        slow = slow!.next; fast = fast.next.next;   // 1x vs 2x speed
        if (slow === fast) return true;              // they meet inside a loop
    }
    return false;
}

Template C · in-place partition — Dutch national flag (sort 0/1/2)

void sortColors(int[] a){
    int low = 0, mid = 0, high = a.length - 1;
    while (mid <= high){
        if (a[mid]==0) swap(a, low++, mid++);
        else if (a[mid]==1) mid++;
        else swap(a, mid, high--);         // 2 → send to the back
    }
}
void sortColors(vector<int>& a){
    int low = 0, mid = 0, high = a.size() - 1;
    while (mid <= high){
        if (a[mid]==0) swap(a[low++], a[mid++]);
        else if (a[mid]==1) mid++;
        else swap(a[mid], a[high--]);         // 2 → send to the back
    }
}
def sort_colors(a):
    low, mid, high = 0, 0, len(a) - 1
    while mid <= high:
        if a[mid] == 0:
            a[low], a[mid] = a[mid], a[low]
            low += 1
            mid += 1
        elif a[mid] == 1:
            mid += 1
        else:
            a[mid], a[high] = a[high], a[mid]        # 2 → send to the back
            high -= 1
function sortColors(a: number[]): void {
    let low = 0, mid = 0, high = a.length - 1;
    while (mid <= high) {
        if (a[mid] === 0) {
            [a[low], a[mid]] = [a[mid], a[low]];
            low++; mid++;
        } else if (a[mid] === 1) {
            mid++;
        } else {
            [a[mid], a[high]] = [a[high], a[mid]];    // 2 → send to the back
            high--;
        }
    }
}

Template D · one pointer per sorted array — count pairs ≤ target

long countPairs(int[] a, int[] b, int target){    // both sorted
    long count = 0; int j = b.length - 1;
    for (int i = 0; i < a.length; i++){
        while (j >= 0 && a[i] + b[j] > target) j--;
        count += (j + 1);                          // b[0..j] all pair with a[i]
    }
    return count;
}
long long countPairs(vector<int>& a, vector<int>& b, int target){    // both sorted
    long long count = 0; int j = b.size() - 1;
    for (int i = 0; i < (int)a.size(); i++){
        while (j >= 0 && a[i] + b[j] > target) j--;
        count += (j + 1);                          // b[0..j] all pair with a[i]
    }
    return count;
}
def count_pairs(a, b, target):    # both sorted
    count = 0
    j = len(b) - 1
    for i in range(len(a)):
        while j >= 0 and a[i] + b[j] > target:
            j -= 1
        count += (j + 1)                          # b[0..j] all pair with a[i]
    return count
function countPairs(a: number[], b: number[], target: number): number {    // both sorted
    let count = 0;
    let j = b.length - 1;
    for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
        while (j >= 0 && a[i] + b[j] > target) j--;
        count += (j + 1);                          // b[0..j] all pair with a[i]
    }
    return count;
}

Worked example — sorted two-sum, target 9 on [2,7,11,15]

lohia[lo]+a[hi]vs 9move
032+15=17>hi→2
022+11=13>hi→1
012+7=9=return [0,1]

Three steps instead of the six pairs a double loop would test. Each pointer only moves one direction, so total work is linear.

Finding the cycle's entry: after fast/slow meet, reset one pointer to the head and advance both at 1x — they meet at the loop start (Floyd's second phase).

11 · Sliding Window

Flowchart leaf: maintain a valid window (subarray/substring)

The idea

For "longest/shortest/count of contiguous subarray or substring with property P", keep a window [left, right]. Grow right to include new elements; when the window violates P, shrink from left until valid again. Each index enters and leaves at most once → O(n). Works when extending never fixes a violation (i.e., the property is monotonic in window size).

The universal shape

int left = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < n; right++){
    add(a[right]);                 // extend window
    while (invalid()){ remove(a[left]); left++; }   // shrink to restore validity
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);        // window is valid here
}
int left = 0;
for (int right = 0; right < n; right++){
    add(a[right]);                 // extend window
    while (invalid()){ remove(a[left]); left++; }   // shrink to restore validity
    best = max(best, right - left + 1);              // window is valid here
}
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    add(a[right])                                    # extend window
    while invalid():
        remove(a[left])
        left += 1                                    # shrink to restore validity
    best = max(best, right - left + 1)                # window is valid here
let left = 0;
for (let right = 0; right < n; right++) {
    add(a[right]);                 // extend window
    while (invalid()) { remove(a[left]); left++; }   // shrink to restore validity
    best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);          // window is valid here
}

Brute force — every subarray O(n²) or O(n³)

Enumerate all O(n²) start/end pairs and re-scan each to test P. The window reuses work between adjacent ranges instead of recomputing from scratch.

Template A · variable window — longest substring without repeats O(n)

int lengthOfLongestSubstring(String s){
    int[] last = new int[128]; Arrays.fill(last, -1);
    int left = 0, best = 0;
    for (int right = 0; right < s.length(); right++){
        char c = s.charAt(right);
        if (last[c] >= left) left = last[c] + 1;   // jump past the earlier copy
        last[c] = right;
        best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
    }
    return best;
}
int lengthOfLongestSubstring(string s){
    vector<int> last(128, -1);
    int left = 0, best = 0;
    for (int right = 0; right < (int)s.length(); right++){
        char c = s[right];
        if (last[c] >= left) left = last[c] + 1;   // jump past the earlier copy
        last[c] = right;
        best = max(best, right - left + 1);
    }
    return best;
}
def length_of_longest_substring(s):
    last = [-1] * 128
    left = best = 0
    for right, c in enumerate(s):
        if last[ord(c)] >= left:
            left = last[ord(c)] + 1        # jump past the earlier copy
        last[ord(c)] = right
        best = max(best, right - left + 1)
    return best
function lengthOfLongestSubstring(s: string): number {
    const last = new Array(128).fill(-1);
    let left = 0, best = 0;
    for (let right = 0; right < s.length; right++) {
        const c = s.charCodeAt(right);
        if (last[c] >= left) left = last[c] + 1;   // jump past the earlier copy
        last[c] = right;
        best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
    }
    return best;
}

Template B · fixed window — max sum of size k

int maxSum(int[] a, int k){
    int sum = 0, best;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) sum += a[i];
    best = sum;
    for (int i = k; i < a.length; i++){
        sum += a[i] - a[i-k];        // slide: add new, drop oldest
        best = Math.max(best, sum);
    }
    return best;
}
int maxSum(vector<int>& a, int k){
    int sum = 0, best;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) sum += a[i];
    best = sum;
    for (int i = k; i < (int)a.size(); i++){
        sum += a[i] - a[i-k];        // slide: add new, drop oldest
        best = max(best, sum);
    }
    return best;
}
def max_sum(a, k):
    total = sum(a[:k])
    best = total
    for i in range(k, len(a)):
        total += a[i] - a[i - k]        # slide: add new, drop oldest
        best = max(best, total)
    return best
function maxSum(a: number[], k: number): number {
    let sum = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i < k; i++) sum += a[i];
    let best = sum;
    for (let i = k; i < a.length; i++) {
        sum += a[i] - a[i - k];        // slide: add new, drop oldest
        best = Math.max(best, sum);
    }
    return best;
}

Template C · counting windows — "at most K" trick for "exactly K"

// # subarrays with exactly K distinct = atMost(K) - atMost(K-1)
int atMost(int[] a, int k){
    Map<Integer,Integer> freq = new HashMap<>();
    int left = 0, count = 0;
    for (int right = 0; right < a.length; right++){
        freq.merge(a[right], 1, Integer::sum);
        while (freq.size() > k){
            int v = a[left++];
            if (freq.merge(v, -1, Integer::sum) == 0) freq.remove(v);
        }
        count += right - left + 1;   // # valid windows ending at right
    }
    return count;
}
// # subarrays with exactly K distinct = atMost(K) - atMost(K-1)
int atMost(vector<int>& a, int k){
    unordered_map<int,int> freq;
    int left = 0, count = 0;
    for (int right = 0; right < (int)a.size(); right++){
        freq[a[right]]++;
        while ((int)freq.size() > k){
            int v = a[left++];
            if (--freq[v] == 0) freq.erase(v);
        }
        count += right - left + 1;   // # valid windows ending at right
    }
    return count;
}
# # subarrays with exactly K distinct = at_most(K) - at_most(K-1)
def at_most(a, k):
    freq = {}
    left = count = 0
    for right in range(len(a)):
        freq[a[right]] = freq.get(a[right], 0) + 1
        while len(freq) > k:
            v = a[left]
            left += 1
            freq[v] -= 1
            if freq[v] == 0:
                del freq[v]
        count += right - left + 1   # # valid windows ending at right
    return count
// # subarrays with exactly K distinct = atMost(K) - atMost(K-1)
function atMost(a: number[], k: number): number {
    const freq = new Map<number, number>();
    let left = 0, count = 0;
    for (let right = 0; right < a.length; right++) {
        freq.set(a[right], (freq.get(a[right]) ?? 0) + 1);
        while (freq.size > k) {
            const v = a[left++];
            const c = freq.get(v)! - 1;
            if (c === 0) freq.delete(v); else freq.set(v, c);
        }
        count += right - left + 1;   // # valid windows ending at right
    }
    return count;
}

Worked example — longest no-repeat substring of "abcabcbb"

rightcharleft before → afterwindowbest
0a0→0a1
1b0→0ab2
2c0→0abc3
3a0→1bca3
4b1→2cab3
5c2→3abc3
6b3→5cb3
7b5→7b3

Answer 3. left only ever moves right, so both pointers together do ≤ 2n steps.

12 · Monotonic Stack

Flowchart leaves: nearest greater/smaller (subarray) nearest bounds (max/min)

The idea

To find, for each element, its nearest greater (or smaller) neighbour, keep a stack whose values stay sorted. When a new element breaks the order, pop the violators — the new element is precisely their "next greater/smaller". Each index is pushed and popped once → O(n). Powers next-greater-element, daily temperatures, largest rectangle, trapping rain water, and stock spans.

Brute force — scan forward for each element O(n²)

For every i, walk right until you find a larger value. The stack remembers unresolved candidates so each is settled in O(1) amortized instead of re-scanned.

Template A · next greater element (days until warmer) O(n)

int[] dailyTemperatures(int[] t){
    int n = t.length; int[] res = new int[n];
    Deque<Integer> st = new ArrayDeque<>();   // indices, temps decreasing down the stack
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++){
        while (!st.isEmpty() && t[i] > t[st.peek()]){
            int j = st.pop();
            res[j] = i - j;                   // i is j's next-warmer day
        }
        st.push(i);
    }
    return res;                               // unresolved stay 0
}
vector<int> dailyTemperatures(vector<int>& t){
    int n = t.size(); vector<int> res(n, 0);
    deque<int> st;   // indices, temps decreasing down the stack
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++){
        while (!st.empty() && t[i] > t[st.back()]){
            int j = st.back(); st.pop_back();
            res[j] = i - j;                   // i is j's next-warmer day
        }
        st.push_back(i);
    }
    return res;                               // unresolved stay 0
}
def daily_temperatures(t):
    n = len(t)
    res = [0] * n
    st = []                                    # indices, temps decreasing down the stack
    for i in range(n):
        while st and t[i] > t[st[-1]]:
            j = st.pop()
            res[j] = i - j                     # i is j's next-warmer day
        st.append(i)
    return res                                 # unresolved stay 0
function dailyTemperatures(t: number[]): number[] {
    const n = t.length;
    const res = new Array(n).fill(0);
    const st: number[] = [];   // indices, temps decreasing down the stack
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        while (st.length && t[i] > t[st[st.length - 1]]) {
            const j = st.pop()!;
            res[j] = i - j;                   // i is j's next-warmer day
        }
        st.push(i);
    }
    return res;                               // unresolved stay 0
}

Template B · largest rectangle in a histogram O(n)

int largestRectangleArea(int[] h){
    int n = h.length, best = 0;
    Deque<Integer> st = new ArrayDeque<>();
    for (int i = 0; i <= n; i++){
        int cur = (i == n) ? 0 : h[i];          // sentinel flushes the stack
        while (!st.isEmpty() && h[st.peek()] >= cur){
            int height = h[st.pop()];
            int width  = st.isEmpty() ? i : i - st.peek() - 1;
            best = Math.max(best, height * width);
        }
        st.push(i);
    }
    return best;
}
int largestRectangleArea(vector<int>& h){
    int n = h.size(), best = 0;
    deque<int> st;
    for (int i = 0; i <= n; i++){
        int cur = (i == n) ? 0 : h[i];          // sentinel flushes the stack
        while (!st.empty() && h[st.back()] >= cur){
            int height = h[st.back()]; st.pop_back();
            int width  = st.empty() ? i : i - st.back() - 1;
            best = max(best, height * width);
        }
        st.push_back(i);
    }
    return best;
}
def largest_rectangle_area(h):
    n = len(h)
    best = 0
    st = []
    for i in range(n + 1):
        cur = 0 if i == n else h[i]             # sentinel flushes the stack
        while st and h[st[-1]] >= cur:
            height = h[st.pop()]
            width = i if not st else i - st[-1] - 1
            best = max(best, height * width)
        st.append(i)
    return best
function largestRectangleArea(h: number[]): number {
    const n = h.length;
    let best = 0;
    const st: number[] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i <= n; i++) {
        const cur = (i === n) ? 0 : h[i];          // sentinel flushes the stack
        while (st.length && h[st[st.length - 1]] >= cur) {
            const height = h[st.pop()!];
            const width = st.length === 0 ? i : i - st[st.length - 1] - 1;
            best = Math.max(best, height * width);
        }
        st.push(i);
    }
    return best;
}

Worked example — next warmer day, [73,74,75,71,69,72,76]

i (temp)pops (resolve)stack (indices) after
0 (73)—[0]
1 (74)pop 0 → res[0]=1[1]
2 (75)pop 1 → res[1]=1[2]
3 (71)—[2,3]
4 (69)—[2,3,4]
5 (72)pop 4 → res[4]=1; pop 3 → res[3]=2[2,5]
6 (76)pop 5 → res[5]=1; pop 2 → res[2]=4[6]

Result [1,1,4,2,1,1,0]. Index 2 (75°) waited 4 days for 76°; index 6 never warms so stays 0.

13 · Prefix Sums & Difference Arrays

Flowchart leaf: sum / additive problem

The idea

Precompute cumulative totals so any range sum is a single subtraction: sum(l..r) = P[r+1] - P[l]. Combine with a hash map of seen prefix sums to count subarrays with a target sum in one pass. The dual, a difference array, applies many range-updates in O(1) each and reconstructs the final array at the end.

Brute force — re-sum each range O(n) per query / O(n²) total

Adding up a[l..r] from scratch for every query. Prefix sums pay O(n) once, then answer each range in O(1).

Template A · count subarrays summing to k (prefix + hashmap) O(n)

int subarraySum(int[] nums, int k){
    Map<Integer,Integer> seen = new HashMap<>();
    seen.put(0, 1);                       // empty prefix
    int sum = 0, count = 0;
    for (int x : nums){
        sum += x;
        count += seen.getOrDefault(sum - k, 0);  // a prior prefix closes a k-sum here
        seen.merge(sum, 1, Integer::sum);
    }
    return count;
}
int subarraySum(vector<int>& nums, int k){
    unordered_map<int,int> seen;
    seen[0] = 1;                       // empty prefix
    int sum = 0, count = 0;
    for (int x : nums){
        sum += x;
        count += seen.count(sum - k) ? seen[sum - k] : 0;  // a prior prefix closes a k-sum here
        seen[sum]++;
    }
    return count;
}
def subarray_sum(nums, k):
    seen = {0: 1}                       # empty prefix
    total = count = 0
    for x in nums:
        total += x
        count += seen.get(total - k, 0)  # a prior prefix closes a k-sum here
        seen[total] = seen.get(total, 0) + 1
    return count
function subarraySum(nums: number[], k: number): number {
    const seen = new Map<number, number>();
    seen.set(0, 1);                       // empty prefix
    let sum = 0, count = 0;
    for (const x of nums) {
        sum += x;
        count += seen.get(sum - k) ?? 0;  // a prior prefix closes a k-sum here
        seen.set(sum, (seen.get(sum) ?? 0) + 1);
    }
    return count;
}

Template B · 2D prefix sum (sub-matrix sum in O(1))

// P[i+1][j+1] = sum of the rectangle from (0,0) to (i,j)
int[][] P = new int[m+1][n+1];
for (int i=0;i<m;i++)
    for (int j=0;j<n;j++)
        P[i+1][j+1] = a[i][j] + P[i][j+1] + P[i+1][j] - P[i][j];
// sum of rows [r1,r2], cols [c1,c2]:
int sum = P[r2+1][c2+1] - P[r1][c2+1] - P[r2+1][c1] + P[r1][c1];
// P[i+1][j+1] = sum of the rectangle from (0,0) to (i,j)
vector<vector<int>> P(m+1, vector<int>(n+1, 0));
for (int i=0;i<m;i++)
    for (int j=0;j<n;j++)
        P[i+1][j+1] = a[i][j] + P[i][j+1] + P[i+1][j] - P[i][j];
// sum of rows [r1,r2], cols [c1,c2]:
int sum = P[r2+1][c2+1] - P[r1][c2+1] - P[r2+1][c1] + P[r1][c1];
# P[i+1][j+1] = sum of the rectangle from (0,0) to (i,j)
P = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
for i in range(m):
    for j in range(n):
        P[i+1][j+1] = a[i][j] + P[i][j+1] + P[i+1][j] - P[i][j]
# sum of rows [r1,r2], cols [c1,c2]:
total = P[r2+1][c2+1] - P[r1][c2+1] - P[r2+1][c1] + P[r1][c1]
// P[i+1][j+1] = sum of the rectangle from (0,0) to (i,j)
const P: number[][] = Array.from({ length: m + 1 }, () => new Array(n + 1).fill(0));
for (let i = 0; i < m; i++)
    for (let j = 0; j < n; j++)
        P[i+1][j+1] = a[i][j] + P[i][j+1] + P[i+1][j] - P[i][j];
// sum of rows [r1,r2], cols [c1,c2]:
const sum = P[r2+1][c2+1] - P[r1][c2+1] - P[r2+1][c1] + P[r1][c1];

Template C · difference array (range add in O(1))

int[] diff = new int[n+1];
void addRange(int l, int r, int val){ diff[l] += val; diff[r+1] -= val; }
