Worked example — 3Sum (LC 15, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Bloomberg

Problem. Given an integer array, return all unique triplets [a, b, c] such that a + b + c == 0.

Idea. Sort the array first. Fix each element nums[i] as the smallest of the triplet, then run the classic two-pointer sweep on the remaining sorted suffix to find pairs summing to -nums[i], skipping duplicate values at every level to keep results unique.

class Solution {
    public List<List<Integer>> threeSum(int[] nums) {
        Arrays.sort(nums);
        List<List<Integer>> res = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int i = 0; i < nums.length - 2; i++) {
            if (i > 0 && nums[i] == nums[i - 1]) continue;
            if (nums[i] > 0) break;
            int lo = i + 1, hi = nums.length - 1;
            while (lo < hi) {
                int sum = nums[i] + nums[lo] + nums[hi];
                if (sum < 0) {
                    lo++;
                } else if (sum > 0) {
                    hi--;
                } else {
                    res.add(Arrays.asList(nums[i], nums[lo], nums[hi]));
                    lo++;
                    hi--;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[lo] == nums[lo - 1]) lo++;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[hi] == nums[hi + 1]) hi--;
                }
            }
        }
        return res;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    vector<vector<int>> threeSum(vector<int>& nums) {
        sort(nums.begin(), nums.end());
        vector<vector<int>> res;
        for (int i = 0; i < (int)nums.size() - 2; i++) {
            if (i > 0 && nums[i] == nums[i - 1]) continue;
            if (nums[i] > 0) break;
            int lo = i + 1, hi = nums.size() - 1;
            while (lo < hi) {
                int sum = nums[i] + nums[lo] + nums[hi];
                if (sum < 0) {
                    lo++;
                } else if (sum > 0) {
                    hi--;
                } else {
                    res.push_back({nums[i], nums[lo], nums[hi]});
                    lo++;
                    hi--;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[lo] == nums[lo - 1]) lo++;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[hi] == nums[hi + 1]) hi--;
                }
            }
        }
        return res;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def threeSum(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[List[int]]:
        nums.sort()
        res = []
        for i in range(len(nums) - 2):
            if i > 0 and nums[i] == nums[i - 1]:
                continue
            if nums[i] > 0:
                break
            lo, hi = i + 1, len(nums) - 1
            while lo < hi:
                total = nums[i] + nums[lo] + nums[hi]
                if total < 0:
                    lo += 1
                elif total > 0:
                    hi -= 1
                else:
                    res.append([nums[i], nums[lo], nums[hi]])
                    lo += 1
                    hi -= 1
                    while lo < hi and nums[lo] == nums[lo - 1]:
                        lo += 1
                    while lo < hi and nums[hi] == nums[hi + 1]:
                        hi -= 1
        return res
class Solution {
    threeSum(nums: number[]): number[][] {
        nums.sort((a, b) => a - b);
        const res: number[][] = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < nums.length - 2; i++) {
            if (i > 0 && nums[i] === nums[i - 1]) continue;
            if (nums[i] > 0) break;
            let lo = i + 1, hi = nums.length - 1;
            while (lo < hi) {
                const sum = nums[i] + nums[lo] + nums[hi];
                if (sum < 0) {
                    lo++;
                } else if (sum > 0) {
                    hi--;
                } else {
                    res.push([nums[i], nums[lo], nums[hi]]);
                    lo++;
                    hi--;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[lo] === nums[lo - 1]) lo++;
                    while (lo < hi && nums[hi] === nums[hi + 1]) hi--;
                }
            }
        }
        return res;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n²) Space O(1) extra — O(n log n) sort plus an O(n) two-pointer sweep for each of n anchors.

Walkthrough. Input [-1,0,1,2,-1,-4] sorts to [-4,-1,-1,0,1,2]:

i (anchor)lo, hisumaction
i=0 (-4)lo=1(-1), hi=5(2)-3sum<0, lo++ ... no triplet found for -4
i=1 (-1)lo=2(-1), hi=5(2)0record [-1,-1,2], then lo++, hi--
i=1 (-1)lo=3(0), hi=4(1)0record [-1,0,1], lo/hi cross, stop
i=2 (-1)skipped-duplicate of nums[1], continue

Result: [[-1,-1,2], [-1,0,1]].

3 · Sliding Window

Tests whether you can maintain a contiguous subarray/substring incrementally — expanding the right edge and shrinking the left edge in response to a constraint — instead of re-scanning from scratch; the tell is "longest/shortest/max substring or subarray satisfying X".

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern

Worked example — Longest Substring Without Repeating Characters (LC 3, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Bloomberg, Adobe

Problem. Given a string s, find the length of the longest substring without repeating characters.

Idea. Keep a window [left, right] and a set of characters currently inside it. Advance right one step at a time; whenever the new character is already in the window, shrink from left until the duplicate is evicted. The window is always duplicate-free, so its length at every step is a candidate answer.

class Solution {
    public int lengthOfLongestSubstring(String s) {
        Set<Character> window = new HashSet<>();
        int left = 0, best = 0;
        for (int right = 0; right < s.length(); right++) {
            char c = s.charAt(right);
            while (window.contains(c)) {
                window.remove(s.charAt(left));
                left++;
            }
            window.add(c);
            best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
        }
        return best;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    int lengthOfLongestSubstring(string s) {
        unordered_set<char> window;
        int left = 0, best = 0;
        for (int right = 0; right < (int)s.length(); right++) {
            char c = s[right];
            while (window.count(c)) {
                window.erase(s[left]);
                left++;
            }
            window.insert(c);
            best = max(best, right - left + 1);
        }
        return best;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def lengthOfLongestSubstring(self, s: str) -> int:
        window = set()
        left = best = 0
        for right in range(len(s)):
            c = s[right]
            while c in window:
                window.remove(s[left])
                left += 1
            window.add(c)
            best = max(best, right - left + 1)
        return best
class Solution {
    lengthOfLongestSubstring(s: string): number {
        const window = new Set<string>();
        let left = 0, best = 0;
        for (let right = 0; right < s.length; right++) {
            const c = s[right];
            while (window.has(c)) {
                window.delete(s[left]);
                left++;
            }
            window.add(c);
            best = Math.max(best, right - left + 1);
        }
        return best;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(min(n, alphabet)) — left and right each advance at most n times total; the set holds at most one entry per distinct character in the window.

Walkthrough. Input "abcabcbb":

rightcharwindow beforeactionbest
0a{}add a1
1b{a}add b2
2c{a,b}add c3
3a{a,b,c}duplicate: evict a (left=1), then add a3
4b{b,c,a}duplicate: evict b (left=2), then add b3

Answer: 3 (substring "abc").

4 · Stack

Worked example — Daily Temperatures (LC 739, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Bloomberg, Google

Problem. Given daily temperatures, return for each day the number of days until a warmer temperature, or 0 if none exists.

Idea. Walk left to right keeping a stack of indices whose answer is still unknown, with temperatures in decreasing order down the stack. When the current temperature beats the temperature at the top index, that index's wait is resolved (current index minus stored index); pop and repeat, then push the current index.

class Solution {
    public int[] dailyTemperatures(int[] temperatures) {
        int n = temperatures.length;
        int[] res = new int[n];
        Deque<Integer> stack = new ArrayDeque<>(); // indices, temps decreasing
        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            while (!stack.isEmpty() && temperatures[i] > temperatures[stack.peek()]) {
                int idx = stack.pop();
                res[idx] = i - idx;
            }
            stack.push(i);
        }
        return res;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    vector<int> dailyTemperatures(vector<int>& temperatures) {
        int n = temperatures.size();
        vector<int> res(n, 0);
        vector<int> stack; // indices, temps decreasing
        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            while (!stack.empty() && temperatures[i] > temperatures[stack.back()]) {
                int idx = stack.back();
                stack.pop_back();
                res[idx] = i - idx;
            }
            stack.push_back(i);
        }
        return res;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def dailyTemperatures(self, temperatures: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(temperatures)
        res = [0] * n
        stack = []  # indices, temps decreasing
        for i in range(n):
            while stack and temperatures[i] > temperatures[stack[-1]]:
                idx = stack.pop()
                res[idx] = i - idx
            stack.append(i)
        return res
class Solution {
    dailyTemperatures(temperatures: number[]): number[] {
        const n = temperatures.length;
        const res = new Array(n).fill(0);
        const stack: number[] = []; // indices, temps decreasing
        for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            while (stack.length > 0 && temperatures[i] > temperatures[stack[stack.length - 1]]) {
                const idx = stack.pop()!;
                res[idx] = i - idx;
            }
            stack.push(i);
        }
        return res;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(n) — each index is pushed once and popped at most once.

Walkthrough. Input [73,74,75,71,69,72,76,73]:

676pop 5 -> res[5]=1, pop 2 -> res[2]=4[6]
773-[6,7]

Result: [1,1,4,2,1,1,0,0].

5 · Binary Search

Tests whether you can spot a monotonic "yes/no" boundary — not just in a sorted array but in the space of possible answers — and drive it out in O(log n); the tell is "sorted", "rotated sorted", or "minimize/maximize the smallest value that still works".

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern
1 Binary Search ★ Easy AmazonGoogleMicrosoft classic lo/hi midpoint search
2 Search a 2D Matrix ★ Medium AmazonMetaMicrosoft treat matrix as flattened sorted array
3 Koko Eating Bananas ★ Medium GoogleAmazonBloomberg binary search on the answer (eating speed)

Worked example — Search in Rotated Sorted Array (LC 33, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Bloomberg

Problem. Given a sorted array rotated at an unknown pivot and a target value, return the index of the target in O(log n) time, or -1 if it is not present.

Idea. At every midpoint, one of the two halves [lo, mid] or [mid, hi] must still be internally sorted even though the whole array isn't. Determine which half is sorted by comparing nums[lo] to nums[mid], then check whether the target falls inside that sorted half's value range to decide which side to keep.

class Solution {
    public int search(int[] nums, int target) {
        int lo = 0, hi = nums.length - 1;
        while (lo <= hi) {
            int mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
            if (nums[mid] == target) return mid;
            if (nums[lo] <= nums[mid]) {
                if (nums[lo] <= target && target < nums[mid]) {
                    hi = mid - 1;
                } else {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                }
            } else {
                if (nums[mid] < target && target <= nums[hi]) {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                } else {
                    hi = mid - 1;
                }
            }
        }
        return -1;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    int search(vector<int>& nums, int target) {

                    hi = mid - 1;
                } else {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                }
            } else {
                if (nums[mid] < target && target <= nums[hi]) {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                } else {
                    hi = mid - 1;
                }
            }
        }
        return -1;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def search(self, nums: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        lo, hi = 0, len(nums) - 1
        while lo <= hi:
            mid = lo + (hi - lo) // 2
            if nums[mid] == target:
                return mid
            if nums[lo] <= nums[mid]:
                if nums[lo] <= target < nums[mid]:
                    hi = mid - 1
                else:
                    lo = mid + 1
            else:
                if nums[mid] < target <= nums[hi]:
                    lo = mid + 1
                else:
                    hi = mid - 1
        return -1
class Solution {
    search(nums: number[], target: number): number {
        let lo = 0, hi = nums.length - 1;
        while (lo <= hi) {
            const mid = lo + Math.floor((hi - lo) / 2);
            if (nums[mid] === target) return mid;
            if (nums[lo] <= nums[mid]) {
                if (nums[lo] <= target && target < nums[mid]) {
                    hi = mid - 1;
                } else {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                }
            } else {
                if (nums[mid] < target && target <= nums[hi]) {
                    lo = mid + 1;
                } else {
                    hi = mid - 1;
                }
            }
        }
        return -1;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(log n) Space O(1) — every iteration discards half of the remaining search space.

Worked example — Reorder List (LC 143, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Microsoft.

Problem. Given the head of a singly linked list L0 → L1 → ... → Ln-1 → Ln, reorder it in place to L0 → Ln → L1 → Ln-1 → L2 → Ln-2 → ... You may not modify node values, only rewire next pointers, and you must use O(1) extra space.

Idea. Three classic sub-routines chained together: (1) locate the midpoint with slow/fast pointers, (2) reverse the second half in place, (3) splice the two halves together by alternating one node from each side. Each step is a well-known O(n) primitive, so the composition stays O(n) time, O(1) space.

class ListNode {
    int val;
    ListNode next;
    ListNode(int val) { this.val = val; }
}

class Solution {
    public void reorderList(ListNode head) {
        if (head == null || head.next == null) return;

        // 1. find the middle (slow ends on the last node of the first half)
        ListNode slow = head, fast = head;
        while (fast.next != null && fast.next.next != null) {
            slow = slow.next;
            fast = fast.next.next;
        }

        // 2. split and reverse the second half
        ListNode second = slow.next;
        slow.next = null;
        ListNode prev = null;
        while (second != null) {
            ListNode next = second.next;
            second.next = prev;
            prev = second;
            second = next;
        }

        // 3. merge the two halves alternately
        ListNode first = head, secondHalf = prev;
        while (secondHalf != null) {
            ListNode tmp1 = first.next;
            ListNode tmp2 = secondHalf.next;
            first.next = secondHalf;
            secondHalf.next = tmp1;
            first = tmp1;
            secondHalf = tmp2;
        }
    }
}

struct ListNode {
    int val;
    ListNode *next;
    ListNode(int val) : val(val), next(nullptr) {}
};

class Solution {
public:
    void reorderList(ListNode* head) {
        if (head == nullptr || head->next == nullptr) return;

        // 1. find the middle (slow ends on the last node of the first half)
        ListNode *slow = head, *fast = head;
        while (fast->next != nullptr && fast->next->next != nullptr) {
            slow = slow->next;
            fast = fast->next->next;
        }

        // 2. split and reverse the second half
        ListNode* second = slow->next;
        slow->next = nullptr;
        ListNode* prev = nullptr;
        while (second != nullptr) {
            ListNode* next = second->next;
            second->next = prev;
            prev = second;
            second = next;
        }

        // 3. merge the two halves alternately
        ListNode *first = head, *secondHalf = prev;
        while (secondHalf != nullptr) {
            ListNode* tmp1 = first->next;
            ListNode* tmp2 = secondHalf->next;
            first->next = secondHalf;
            secondHalf->next = tmp1;
            first = tmp1;
            secondHalf = tmp2;
        }
    }
};
class ListNode:
    def __init__(self, val=0):
        self.val = val
        self.next = None

class Solution:
    def reorderList(self, head: ListNode) -> None:
        if head is None or head.next is None:
            return

        # 1. find the middle (slow ends on the last node of the first half)
        slow, fast = head, head
        while fast.next is not None and fast.next.next is not None:
            slow = slow.next
            fast = fast.next.next

        # 2. split and reverse the second half
        second = slow.next
        slow.next = None
        prev = None
        while second is not None:
            next = second.next
            second.next = prev
            prev = second
            second = next

        # 3. merge the two halves alternately
        first, secondHalf = head, prev
        while secondHalf is not None:
            tmp1 = first.next
            tmp2 = secondHalf.next
            first.next = secondHalf
            secondHalf.next = tmp1
            first = tmp1
            secondHalf = tmp2
class ListNode {
    val: number;
    next: ListNode | null;
    constructor(val: number) {
        this.val = val;
        this.next = null;
    }
}

class Solution {
    reorderList(head: ListNode | null): void {
        if (head === null || head.next === null) return;

        // 1. find the middle (slow ends on the last node of the first half)
        let slow: ListNode | null = head, fast: ListNode | null = head;
        while (fast!.next !== null && fast!.next!.next !== null) {
            slow = slow!.next;
            fast = fast!.next!.next;
        }

        // 2. split and reverse the second half
        let second: ListNode | null = slow!.next;
        slow!.next = null;
        let prev: ListNode | null = null;
        while (second !== null) {
            const next: ListNode | null = second.next;
            second.next = prev;
            prev = second;
            second = next;
        }

        // 3. merge the two halves alternately
        let first: ListNode | null = head, secondHalf: ListNode | null = prev;
        while (secondHalf !== null) {
            const tmp1: ListNode | null = first!.next;
            const tmp2: ListNode | null = secondHalf.next;
            first!.next = secondHalf;
            secondHalf.next = tmp1;
            first = tmp1;
            secondHalf = tmp2;
        }
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(1) — a constant number of single passes over the list, all pointer rewiring with no auxiliary structures.

Walkthrough. Input: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5.

StepState
Find middleslow lands on 3, so first half = 1→2→3, second = 4→5
Reverse second half4→5 becomes 5→4
Merge iteration 1first=1, secondHalf=5 → 1→5, first advances to 2
Merge iteration 2first=2, secondHalf=4 → 2→4, first advances to 3
secondHalf exhaustedloop stops; 3's original next (null) already terminates the list
Result1 → 5 → 2 → 4 → 3

Worked example — Validate Binary Search Tree (LC 98, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Microsoft, Apple, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given the root of a binary tree, determine whether it is a valid binary search tree — for every node, all values in its left subtree must be strictly less than the node's value and all values in its right subtree must be strictly greater.

Idea. Checking only left.val < node.val < right.val at each node is insufficient — a node deep in the left subtree could still violate the ancestor's bound. Instead thread a valid (low, high) range down through the recursion: each node must fall strictly inside the range inherited from its ancestors, and it narrows that range for its own children.

class TreeNode {
    int val;
    TreeNode left, right;
    TreeNode(int val) { this.val = val; }
}

class Solution {
    public boolean isValidBST(TreeNode root) {
        return valid(root, null, null);
    }

    private boolean valid(TreeNode node, Integer low, Integer high) {
        if (node == null) return true;
        if ((low != null && node.val <= low) || (high != null && node.val >= high)) {
            return false;
        }
        return valid(node.left, low, node.val) && valid(node.right, node.val, high);
    }
}

struct TreeNode {
    int val;
    TreeNode *left, *right;
    TreeNode(int val) : val(val), left(nullptr), right(nullptr) {}
};

class Solution {
public:
    bool isValidBST(TreeNode* root) {
        return valid(root, std::nullopt, std::nullopt);
    }

private:
    bool valid(TreeNode* node, std::optional<int> low, std::optional<int> high) {
        if (node == nullptr) return true;
        if ((low.has_value() && node->val <= low.value()) || (high.has_value() && node->val >= high.value())) {
            return false;
        }
        return valid(node->left, low, node->val) && valid(node->right, node->val, high);
    }
};
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, val=0):
        self.val = val
        self.left = None
        self.right = None

class Solution:
    def isValidBST(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> bool:
        return self._valid(root, None, None)

    def _valid(self, node, low, high):
        if node is None:
            return True
        if (low is not None and node.val <= low) or (high is not None and node.val >= high):
            return False
        return self._valid(node.left, low, node.val) and self._valid(node.right, node.val, high)
class TreeNode {
    val: number;
    left: TreeNode | null;
    right: TreeNode | null;
    constructor(val: number) {
        this.val = val;
        this.left = null;
        this.right = null;
    }
}

class Solution {
    isValidBST(root: TreeNode | null): boolean {
        return this.valid(root, null, null);
    }

    private valid(node: TreeNode | null, low: number | null, high: number | null): boolean {
        if (node === null) return true;
        if ((low !== null && node.val <= low) || (high !== null && node.val >= high)) {
            return false;
        }
        return this.valid(node.left, low, node.val) && this.valid(node.right, node.val, high);
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(h) — every node is visited once; space is the recursion stack, proportional to tree height h.

Walkthrough. Input tree: root 5, left child 1, right child 4 (whose children are 3 and 6).

CallBounds (low, high)Result
valid(5, null, null)(-∞, +∞)5 in range, recurse both sides
valid(1, null, 5)(-∞, 5)1 < 5, valid leaf
valid(4, 5, null)(5, +∞)4 ≤ 5 → false
Overall—returns false — the right subtree of 5 must contain only values > 5, but 4 breaks that

Worked example — Implement Trie (Prefix Tree) (LC 208, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Google, Microsoft.

Problem. Implement a trie with insert(word), search(word) (exact match), and startsWith(prefix) (prefix match), all operating on lowercase English letters.

Idea. Each trie node holds a fixed-size array of 26 child pointers (one per letter) plus a boolean marking "a word ends here". Insert and search both just walk character by character, either creating or following child links; startsWith is identical to search minus the end-of-word check.

class Trie {
    private Trie[] children;
    private boolean isEnd;

    public Trie() {
        children = new Trie[26];
        isEnd = false;
    }

    public void insert(String word) {
        Trie node = this;
        for (char c : word.toCharArray()) {
            int i = c - 'a';
            if (node.children[i] == null) node.children[i] = new Trie();
            node = node.children[i];
        }
        node.isEnd = true;
    }

    public boolean search(String word) {
        Trie node = find(word);
        return node != null && node.isEnd;
    }

    public boolean startsWith(String prefix) {
        return find(prefix) != null;
    }

    private Trie find(String s) {
        Trie node = this;
        for (char c : s.toCharArray()) {
            int i = c - 'a';
            if (node.children[i] == null) return null;
            node = node.children[i];
        }
        return node;
    }
}

class Trie {
private:
    Trie* children[26];
    bool isEnd;

public:
    Trie() {
        for (int i = 0; i < 26; i++) children[i] = nullptr;
        isEnd = false;
    }

    void insert(string word) {
        Trie* node = this;
        for (char c : word) {
            int i = c - 'a';
            if (node->children[i] == nullptr) node->children[i] = new Trie();
            node = node->children[i];
        }
        node->isEnd = true;
    }

    bool search(string word) {
        Trie* node = find(word);
        return node != nullptr && node->isEnd;
    }

    bool startsWith(string prefix) {
        return find(prefix) != nullptr;
    }

private:
    Trie* find(string s) {
        Trie* node = this;
        for (char c : s) {
            int i = c - 'a';
            if (node->children[i] == nullptr) return nullptr;
            node = node->children[i];
        }
        return node;
    }
};
class Trie:
    def __init__(self):
        self.children = [None] * 26
        self.isEnd = False

    def insert(self, word: str) -> None:
        node = self
        for c in word:
            i = ord(c) - ord('a')
            if node.children[i] is None:
                node.children[i] = Trie()
            node = node.children[i]
        node.isEnd = True

    def search(self, word: str) -> bool:
        node = self._find(word)
        return node is not None and node.isEnd

    def startsWith(self, prefix: str) -> bool:
        return self._find(prefix) is not None

    def _find(self, s: str):
        node = self
        for c in s:
            i = ord(c) - ord('a')
            if node.children[i] is None:
                return None
            node = node.children[i]
        return node
class Trie {
    private children: (Trie | null)[];
    private isEnd: boolean;

    constructor() {
        this.children = new Array(26).fill(null);
        this.isEnd = false;
    }

    insert(word: string): void {
        let node: Trie = this;
        for (const c of word) {
            const i = c.charCodeAt(0) - 'a'.charCodeAt(0);
            if (node.children[i] === null) node.children[i] = new Trie();
            node = node.children[i]!;
        }
        node.isEnd = true;
    }

    search(word: string): boolean {
        const node = this.find(word);
        return node !== null && node.isEnd;
    }

    startsWith(prefix: string): boolean {
        return this.find(prefix) !== null;
    }

    private find(s: string): Trie | null {
        let node: Trie = this;
        for (const c of s) {
            const i = c.charCodeAt(0) - 'a'.charCodeAt(0);
            if (node.children[i] === null) return null;
            node = node.children[i]!;
        }
        return node;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(L) Space O(N·L) — each call costs the length L of the word/prefix; total space is proportional to the number of inserted words N times their average length (bounded by shared prefixes).

Walkthrough. Operations: insert("apple"), search("app"), startsWith("app"), insert("app"), search("app").

CallWhat happensReturns
insert("apple")creates chain a-p-p-l-e, marks 'e' node isEnd=true—
search("app")walks a-p-p, reaches a real node but isEnd is false therefalse
startsWith("app")walks a-p-p, node exists regardless of isEndtrue
insert("app")walks existing a-p-p node, now marks its isEnd=true—
search("app")walks a-p-p, isEnd is now truetrue

Worked example — Kth Largest Element in an Array (LC 215, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Google.

Problem. Given an integer array nums and an integer k, return the kth largest element in the array (counting from the largest, so k=1 is the maximum), without fully sorting the array.

Idea. Maintain a min-heap capped at size k. Push every number; whenever the heap grows past k, pop the smallest. After processing all n numbers, the heap holds exactly the k largest elements seen, and its smallest member (the top of a min-heap) is the kth largest overall.

import java.util.PriorityQueue;

class Solution {
    public int findKthLargest(int[] nums, int k) {
        PriorityQueue<Integer> minHeap = new PriorityQueue<>();
        for (int n : nums) {
            minHeap.offer(n);
            if (minHeap.size() > k) {
                minHeap.poll();
            }
        }
        return minHeap.peek();
    }
}

#include <queue>

class Solution {
public:
    int findKthLargest(vector<int>& nums, int k) {
        priority_queue<int, vector<int>, greater<int>> minHeap;
        for (int n : nums) {
            minHeap.push(n);
            if (minHeap.size() > k) {
                minHeap.pop();
            }
        }
        return minHeap.top();
    }
};
import heapq

class Solution:
    def findKthLargest(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        minHeap = []
        for n in nums:
            heapq.heappush(minHeap, n)
            if len(minHeap) > k:
                heapq.heappop(minHeap)
        return minHeap[0]
class Solution {
    findKthLargest(nums: number[], k: number): number {
        const minHeap: number[] = [];
        for (const n of nums) {
            minHeap.push(n);
            minHeap.sort((a, b) => a - b);
            if (minHeap.length > k) {
                minHeap.shift();
            }
        }
        return minHeap[0];
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n log k) Space O(k) — each of n offers/polls costs O(log k) on a heap bounded to size k; a quickselect partition alternative achieves average O(n) time with O(1) extra space.

Walkthrough. Input: nums = [3,2,1,5,6,4], k = 2.

10 · Backtracking

Tests systematic exploration of a decision tree with pruning; reach for it when the problem asks for "all possible" subsets, permutations, combinations, or valid placements and a brute-force search with undo is feasible.

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern
1 Subsets ★ Medium AmazonMetaGoogle include/exclude recursion
2 Combination Sum ★ Medium AmazonSnowflakeUber unbounded pick, same-index revisit
3 Permutations Medium MicrosoftAmazonBloomberg swap-in-place / used[] array
4 Subsets II Medium MetaAmazon sort + skip duplicate siblings
5 Combination Sum II Medium AmazonMeta sort + skip duplicate siblings, bounded pick
6 Word Search ★ Medium MicrosoftAmazonBloomberg grid DFS with visited-mark/unmark
7 Palindrome Partitioning Medium AmazonMeta prefix-check + recurse on suffix
8 Letter Combinations of a Phone Number ★ Medium AmazonUberGoogle index-driven cartesian product
9 N-Queens Hard AmazonMicrosoftZenefits row-by-row placement + column/diagonal sets

Worked example — Subsets (LC 78, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Google, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given an integer array nums of unique elements, return all possible subsets (the power set), in any order. Input [1,2,3] produces 8 subsets including [] and [1,2,3].

Idea. Every element is either included or excluded from the current subset. Walk the array index by index, at each index branch into "take it" and "skip it", and record the running subset (a copy) at every recursive call — that alone visits all 2^n leaves of the decision tree. Undo the choice (backtrack) after each branch so the shared list is reused correctly across siblings.

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

class Solution {
    public List<List<Integer>> subsets(int[] nums) {
        List<List<Integer>> result = new ArrayList<>();
        List<Integer> current = new ArrayList<>();
        backtrack(nums, 0, current, result);
        return result;
    }

    private void backtrack(int[] nums, int index, List<Integer> current,
                            List<List<Integer>> result) {
        if (index == nums.length) {
            result.add(new ArrayList<>(current));
            return;
        }

        // Branch 1: exclude nums[index]
        backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);

        // Branch 2: include nums[index]
        current.add(nums[index]);
        backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);
        current.remove(current.size() - 1); // undo
    }
}

#include <vector>

class Solution {
public:
    vector<vector<int>> subsets(vector<int>& nums) {
        vector<vector<int>> result;
        vector<int> current;
        backtrack(nums, 0, current, result);
        return result;
    }

private:
    void backtrack(vector<int>& nums, int index, vector<int>& current,
                    vector<vector<int>>& result) {
        if (index == (int)nums.size()) {
            result.push_back(current);
            return;
        }

        // Branch 1: exclude nums[index]
        backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);

        // Branch 2: include nums[index]
        current.push_back(nums[index]);
        backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);
        current.pop_back(); // undo
    }
};
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsets(self, nums: List[int]) -> List[List[int]]:
        result = []
        current = []
        self._backtrack(nums, 0, current, result)
        return result

    def _backtrack(self, nums, index, current, result):
        if index == len(nums):
            result.append(list(current))
            return

        # Branch 1: exclude nums[index]
        self._backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result)

        # Branch 2: include nums[index]
        current.append(nums[index])
        self._backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result)
        current.pop()  # undo
class Solution {
    subsets(nums: number[]): number[][] {
        const result: number[][] = [];
        const current: number[] = [];
        this.backtrack(nums, 0, current, result);
        return result;
    }

    private backtrack(nums: number[], index: number, current: number[],
                       result: number[][]): void {
        if (index === nums.length) {
            result.push([...current]);
            return;
        }

        // Branch 1: exclude nums[index]
        this.backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);

        // Branch 2: include nums[index]
        current.push(nums[index]);
        this.backtrack(nums, index + 1, current, result);
        current.pop(); // undo
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n * 2^n) Space O(n) — 2^n subsets are produced, each costing O(n) to copy; recursion depth is n.

Walkthrough. nums = [1,2]. index=0: exclude branch recurses with index=1, current=[]; there, exclude branch reaches index=2 → record []; include branch pushes 2 → current=[2] → record [2], then pops 2. Back at index=0's include branch, push 1 → current=[1], recurse to index=1: exclude → record [1]; include 2 → current=[1,2] → record [1,2], pop 2, pop 1. Final result: [[], [2], [1], [1,2]].

Worked example — Course Schedule (LC 207, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Meta, Google, Bloomberg.

Problem. There are numCourses courses labeled 0..numCourses-1. prerequisites[i] = [a, b] means you must take course b before course a. Return true if it is possible to finish all courses (i.e. the prerequisite graph has no cycle).

Idea. Model prerequisites as a directed graph and compute a topological order with Kahn's algorithm: track in-degree (number of unmet prerequisites) per course, seed a queue with all zero-in-degree courses, and repeatedly remove a course, decrementing its neighbors' in-degrees, pushing any that hit zero. If every course gets processed, there is no cycle and the schedule is feasible; if some remain stuck with positive in-degree, a cycle blocks them.

import java.util.ArrayDeque;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Queue;

class Solution {
    public boolean canFinish(int numCourses, int[][] prerequisites) {
        List<List<Integer>> graph = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int i = 0; i < numCourses; i++) {
            graph.add(new ArrayList<>());
        }

        int[] inDegree = new int[numCourses];
        for (int[] pre : prerequisites) {
            int course = pre[0];
            int prereq = pre[1];
            graph.get(prereq).add(course); // prereq -> course
            inDegree[course]++;
        }

        Queue<Integer> queue = new ArrayDeque<>();
        for (int i = 0; i < numCourses; i++) {
            if (inDegree[i] == 0) {
                queue.offer(i);
            }
        }

        int processed = 0;
        while (!queue.isEmpty()) {
            int course = queue.poll();
            processed++;
            for (int next : graph.get(course)) {
                inDegree[next]--;
                if (inDegree[next] == 0) {
                    queue.offer(next);
                }
            }
        }

        return processed == numCourses;
    }
}

#include <vector>
#include <queue>

class Solution {
public:
    bool canFinish(int numCourses, vector<vector<int>>& prerequisites) {
        vector<vector<int>> graph(numCourses);

        vector<int> inDegree(numCourses, 0);
        for (auto& pre : prerequisites) {
            int course = pre[0];
            int prereq = pre[1];
            graph[prereq].push_back(course); // prereq -> course
            inDegree[course]++;
        }

        queue<int> q;
        for (int i = 0; i < numCourses; i++) {
            if (inDegree[i] == 0) {
                q.push(i);
            }
        }

        int processed = 0;
        while (!q.empty()) {
            int course = q.front();
            q.pop();
            processed++;
            for (int next : graph[course]) {
                inDegree[next]--;
                if (inDegree[next] == 0) {
                    q.push(next);
                }
            }
        }

        return processed == numCourses;
    }
};
from collections import deque
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def canFinish(self, numCourses: int, prerequisites: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(numCourses)]

        inDegree = [0] * numCourses
        for pre in prerequisites:
            course = pre[0]
            prereq = pre[1]
            graph[prereq].append(course)  # prereq -> course
            inDegree[course] += 1

        queue = deque()
        for i in range(numCourses):
            if inDegree[i] == 0:
                queue.append(i)

        processed = 0
        while queue:
            course = queue.popleft()
            processed += 1
            for next in graph[course]:
                inDegree[next] -= 1
                if inDegree[next] == 0:
                    queue.append(next)

        return processed == numCourses
class Solution {
    canFinish(numCourses: number, prerequisites: number[][]): boolean {
        const graph: number[][] = Array.from({ length: numCourses }, () => []);

        const inDegree = new Array(numCourses).fill(0);
        for (const pre of prerequisites) {
            const course = pre[0];
            const prereq = pre[1];
            graph[prereq].push(course); // prereq -> course
            inDegree[course]++;
        }

        const queue: number[] = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < numCourses; i++) {
            if (inDegree[i] === 0) {
                queue.push(i);
            }
        }

        let processed = 0;
        while (queue.length > 0) {
            const course = queue.shift()!;
            processed++;
            for (const next of graph[course]) {
                inDegree[next]--;
                if (inDegree[next] === 0) {
                    queue.push(next);
                }
            }
        }

        return processed === numCourses;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(V + E) Space O(V + E) — each course and each edge is visited exactly once during graph build and BFS.

Walkthrough. numCourses=4, prerequisites=[[1,0],[2,0],[3,1],[3,2]]. Graph: 0->{1,2}, 1->{3}, 2->{3}. inDegree=[0,1,1,2]. Queue starts with [0]. Pop 0 (processed=1): decrement 1→0 (enqueue), decrement 2→0 (enqueue). Pop 1 (processed=2): decrement 3→1. Pop 2 (processed=3): decrement 3→0 (enqueue). Pop 3 (processed=4). processed==4==numCourses → return true.

Worked example — Network Delay Time (LC 743, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Google, Amazon, Uber, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given n nodes labeled 1..n and directed weighted edges times[i] = [u, v, w] (signal travels u→v in w time), plus a source node k, return the time for all n nodes to receive a signal sent from k, or -1 if some node is unreachable.

Idea. This is exactly single-source shortest paths on a non-negative weighted directed graph, so run Dijkstra's algorithm: maintain a min-heap of (distance, node) pairs, always expand the currently-closest unvisited node, and relax its outgoing edges. The answer is the maximum finite distance across all nodes (the last node to receive the signal); if any node's distance is never finalized, return -1.

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.PriorityQueue;

class Solution {
    public int networkDelayTime(int[][] times, int n, int k) {
        List<List<int[]>> graph = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int i = 0; i <= n; i++) {
            graph.add(new ArrayList<>());
        }
        for (int[] t : times) {
            int u = t[0], v = t[1], w = t[2];
            graph.get(u).add(new int[]{v, w});
        }

        int[] dist = new int[n + 1];
        java.util.Arrays.fill(dist, Integer.MAX_VALUE);
        dist[k] = 0;

        // {distance, node}
        PriorityQueue<int[]> pq = new PriorityQueue<>((a, b) -> a[0] - b[0]);
        pq.offer(new int[]{0, k});
        boolean[] visited = new boolean[n + 1];

        while (!pq.isEmpty()) {
            int[] curr = pq.poll();
            int d = curr[0], node = curr[1];
            if (visited[node]) continue;
            visited[node] = true;

            for (int[] edge : graph.get(node)) {
                int next = edge[0], weight = edge[1];
                if (!visited[next] && d + weight < dist[next]) {
                    dist[next] = d + weight;
                    pq.offer(new int[]{dist[next], next});
                }
            }
        }

        int maxDist = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            if (dist[i] == Integer.MAX_VALUE) {
                return -1;
            }
            maxDist = Math.max(maxDist, dist[i]);
        }
        return maxDist;
    }
}

#include <vector>
#include <queue>
#include <climits>

class Solution {
public:
    int networkDelayTime(vector<vector<int>>& times, int n, int k) {
        vector<vector<pair<int,int>>> graph(n + 1);
        for (auto& t : times) {
            int u = t[0], v = t[1], w = t[2];
            graph[u].push_back({v, w});
        }

        vector<int> dist(n + 1, INT_MAX);
        dist[k] = 0;

        // {distance, node}
        priority_queue<pair<int,int>, vector<pair<int,int>>, greater<pair<int,int>>> pq;
        pq.push({0, k});
        vector<bool> visited(n + 1, false);

        while (!pq.empty()) {
            auto curr = pq.top(); pq.pop();
            int d = curr.first, node = curr.second;
            if (visited[node]) continue;
            visited[node] = true;

            for (auto& edge : graph[node]) {
                int next = edge.first, weight = edge.second;
                if (!visited[next] && d + weight < dist[next]) {
                    dist[next] = d + weight;
                    pq.push({dist[next], next});
                }
            }
        }

        int maxDist = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            if (dist[i] == INT_MAX) {
                return -1;
            }
            maxDist = max(maxDist, dist[i]);
        }
        return maxDist;
    }
};
import heapq
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def networkDelayTime(self, times: List[List[int]], n: int, k: int) -> int:
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
        for t in times:
            u, v, w = t[0], t[1], t[2]
            graph[u].append((v, w))

        dist = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
        dist[k] = 0

        # (distance, node)
        pq = [(0, k)]
        visited = [False] * (n + 1)

        while pq:
            d, node = heapq.heappop(pq)
            if visited[node]:
                continue
            visited[node] = True

            for next, weight in graph[node]:
                if not visited[next] and d + weight < dist[next]:
                    dist[next] = d + weight
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (dist[next], next))

        maxDist = 0
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            if dist[i] == float('inf'):
                return -1
            maxDist = max(maxDist, dist[i])
        return maxDist
class Solution {
    networkDelayTime(times: number[][], n: number, k: number): number {
        const graph: [number, number][][] = Array.from({ length: n + 1 }, () => []);
        for (const t of times) {
            const [u, v, w] = t;
            graph[u].push([v, w]);
        }

        const dist = new Array(n + 1).fill(Infinity);
        dist[k] = 0;

        // [distance, node]
        let pq: [number, number][] = [[0, k]];
        const visited = new Array(n + 1).fill(false);

        while (pq.length > 0) {
            pq.sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
            const [d, node] = pq.shift()!;
            if (visited[node]) continue;
            visited[node] = true;

            for (const [next, weight] of graph[node]) {
                if (!visited[next] && d + weight < dist[next]) {
                    dist[next] = d + weight;
                    pq.push([dist[next], next]);
                }
            }
        }

        let maxDist = 0;
        for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            if (dist[i] === Infinity) {
                return -1;
            }
            maxDist = Math.max(maxDist, dist[i]);
        }
        return maxDist;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(E log V) Space O(V + E) — each edge triggers at most one heap insertion, and the heap holds up to E entries.

Walkthrough. times=[[2,1,1],[2,3,1],[3,4,1]], n=4, k=2. dist=[_,MAX,0,MAX,MAX]. Pop (0,2): relax 1→dist[1]=1, push (1,1); relax 3→dist[3]=1, push (1,3). Pop (1,1): node 1 has no outgoing edges. Pop (1,3): relax 4→dist[4]=2, push (2,4). Pop (2,4): no outgoing edges. Final dist=[_,1,0,1,2]; max = 2 → return 2.

Worked example — Coin Change (LC 322, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Google, Uber, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given an array coins of distinct denominations and an integer amount, return the fewest number of coins needed to make up amount. If it cannot be made, return -1. Each coin can be used unlimited times.

Idea. Let dp[a] = minimum coins to form amount a, with dp[0] = 0. For every amount a from 1 up to the target, try every coin c ≤ a and take dp[a] = min(dp[a], dp[a - c] + 1). This is unbounded knapsack: each coin denomination can be reused, so the inner loop simply scans all coins for every amount rather than tracking how many times a coin was used.

class Solution {
    public int coinChange(int[] coins, int amount) {
        int[] dp = new int[amount + 1];
        Arrays.fill(dp, amount + 1); // sentinel > any real answer
        dp[0] = 0;
        for (int a = 1; a <= amount; a++) {
            for (int c : coins) {
                if (c <= a) {
                    dp[a] = Math.min(dp[a], dp[a - c] + 1);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[amount] > amount ? -1 : dp[amount];
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    int coinChange(vector<int>& coins, int amount) {
        vector<int> dp(amount + 1, amount + 1); // sentinel > any real answer
        dp[0] = 0;
        for (int a = 1; a <= amount; a++) {
            for (int c : coins) {
                if (c <= a) {
                    dp[a] = min(dp[a], dp[a - c] + 1);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[amount] > amount ? -1 : dp[amount];
    }
};
class Solution:
    def coinChange(self, coins: List[int], amount: int) -> int:
        dp = [amount + 1] * (amount + 1)  # sentinel > any real answer
        dp[0] = 0
        for a in range(1, amount + 1):
            for c in coins:
                if c <= a:
                    dp[a] = min(dp[a], dp[a - c] + 1)
        return -1 if dp[amount] > amount else dp[amount]
class Solution {
    coinChange(coins: number[], amount: number): number {
        const dp = new Array(amount + 1).fill(amount + 1); // sentinel > any real answer
        dp[0] = 0;
        for (let a = 1; a <= amount; a++) {
            for (const c of coins) {
                if (c <= a) {
                    dp[a] = Math.min(dp[a], dp[a - c] + 1);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[amount] > amount ? -1 : dp[amount];
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(amount × coins.length) Space O(amount) — one dp array, nested loop over every amount and every coin.

Walkthrough. coins = [1, 2, 5], amount = 11. Filling dp[0..11] left to right:

a01234567891011
dp[a]011221223323

Each cell picks the best of "add a 1", "add a 2", or "add a 5" to a smaller already-solved amount — e.g. dp[10] = dp[5] + 1 = 2 (two 5s), and dp[11] = dp[10] + 1 = 3 (5 + 5 + 1). Final answer dp[11] = 3.

Worked example — Longest Common Subsequence (LC 1143, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Google, Microsoft, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given two strings text1 and text2, return the length of their longest common subsequence (characters in relative order, not necessarily contiguous). If there is none, return 0.

Idea. Let dp[i][j] = LCS length of text1[0..i) and text2[0..j). If the last characters match, extend the diagonal: dp[i][j] = dp[i-1][j-1] + 1. Otherwise the match must skip one character from either string: dp[i][j] = max(dp[i-1][j], dp[i][j-1]).

class Solution {
    public int longestCommonSubsequence(String text1, String text2) {
        int m = text1.length(), n = text2.length();
        int[][] dp = new int[m + 1][n + 1];
        for (int i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
            for (int j = 1; j <= n; j++) {
                if (text1.charAt(i - 1) == text2.charAt(j - 1)) {
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
                } else {
                    dp[i][j] = Math.max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[m][n];
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    int longestCommonSubsequence(string text1, string text2) {
        int m = text1.length(), n = text2.length();
        vector<vector<int>> dp(m + 1, vector<int>(n + 1, 0));
        for (int i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
            for (int j = 1; j <= n; j++) {
                if (text1[i - 1] == text2[j - 1]) {
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
                } else {
                    dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[m][n];
    }
};
class Solution:
    def longestCommonSubsequence(self, text1: str, text2: str) -> int:
        m, n = len(text1), len(text2)
        dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)]
        for i in range(1, m + 1):
            for j in range(1, n + 1):
                if text1[i - 1] == text2[j - 1]:
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1
                else:
                    dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1])
        return dp[m][n]
class Solution {
    longestCommonSubsequence(text1: string, text2: string): number {
        const m = text1.length, n = text2.length;
        const dp: number[][] = Array.from({ length: m + 1 }, () => new Array(n + 1).fill(0));
        for (let i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
            for (let j = 1; j <= n; j++) {
                if (text1[i - 1] === text2[j - 1]) {
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
                } else {
                    dp[i][j] = Math.max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
                }
            }
        }
        return dp[m][n];
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(m × n) Space O(m × n) — one cell per pair of prefixes; reducible to O(min(m,n)) with two rolling rows.

Walkthrough. text1 = "abcde", text2 = "ace". Filled grid (rows = text1 prefix length, cols = text2 prefix length):

i \ j""ace
""0000
a0111
b0111
c0122
d0122
e0123

The diagonal bumps happen at matching pairs ('a','a'), ('c','c'), ('e','e'), giving dp[5][3] = 3 for the subsequence "ace".

15 · Greedy

Tests whether a locally optimal choice at each step provably leads to a globally optimal solution — the tell is an exchange argument or "process in sorted order and commit, never revisit" phrasing.

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern
1 Maximum Subarray ★ Medium AmazonMicrosoftLinkedIn Kadane's running max
2 Jump Game ★ Medium AmazonGoogleFacebook track reachable frontier
3 Jump Game II Medium AmazonGoogle min jumps via greedy level expansion
4 Gas Station Medium AmazonGoogle prefix-sum reset on deficit
5 Hand of Straights Medium Amazon counting map + greedy run grouping
6 Merge Triplets to Form Target Triplet Medium Amazon component-wise greedy filter
7 Partition Labels Medium AmazonGoogle last-index greedy window growth
8 Valid Parenthesis String Medium FacebookAmazon track range of possible open-counts

Worked example — Maximum Subarray, Kadane's Algorithm (LC 53, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Microsoft, LinkedIn, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given an integer array nums, find the contiguous subarray with the largest sum and return that sum.

Idea. Greedily track curSum, the best sum of a subarray ending at the current index. At each step, either extend the previous run or restart from the current element — whichever is larger: curSum = max(nums[i], curSum + nums[i]). A negative running sum never helps a future subarray, so it's always correct to drop it and restart. Track the best curSum ever seen.

class Solution {
    public int maxSubArray(int[] nums) {
        int curSum = nums[0];
        int best = nums[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
            curSum = Math.max(nums[i], curSum + nums[i]);
            best = Math.max(best, curSum);
        }
        return best;
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    int maxSubArray(vector<int>& nums) {
        int curSum = nums[0];
        int best = nums[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < (int)nums.size(); i++) {
            curSum = max(nums[i], curSum + nums[i]);
            best = max(best, curSum);
        }
        return best;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def maxSubArray(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        curSum = nums[0]
        best = nums[0]
        for i in range(1, len(nums)):
            curSum = max(nums[i], curSum + nums[i])
            best = max(best, curSum)
        return best

    maxSubArray(nums: number[]): number {

        let best = nums[0];
        for (let i = 1; i < nums.length; i++) {
            curSum = Math.max(nums[i], curSum + nums[i]);

        }
        return best;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(1) — single left-to-right pass, two scalars.

i012345678
nums[i]-21-34-121-54
curSum-21-2435615
best-211445666

The running sum resets at index 2 (dropping the -2 prefix) and peaks at 6 by index 6, covering the subarray [4, -1, 2, 1]. Answer: 6.

16 · Intervals

Tests handling of overlapping ranges — the tell is "sort by start or end, then sweep once" to merge, count conflicts, or schedule resources.

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern
1 Insert Interval ★ Medium GoogleFacebookLinkedIn merge while inserting into sorted list
2 Merge Intervals ★ Medium FacebookAmazonGoogle sort by start, sweep and merge
3 Non-overlapping Intervals Medium AmazonFacebook sort by end, greedy keep earliest finish
4 Meeting Rooms Easy FacebookGoogleAmazon sort + adjacent overlap check
5 Meeting Rooms II ★ Medium FacebookGoogleAmazon min-heap of active end times
6 Minimum Interval to Include Each Query Hard Google offline sort + min-heap sweep

Worked example — Merge Intervals (LC 56, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Facebook, Amazon, Google, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given an array of intervals where intervals[i] = [start_i, end_i], merge all overlapping intervals and return an array of the non-overlapping intervals that cover all the input ranges.

Idea. Sort intervals by start time. Then sweep once: if the current interval's start is beyond the end of the last merged interval, it starts a new group; otherwise it overlaps, so extend the last merged interval's end to the max of the two. Sorting is what makes a single linear sweep sufficient — the greedy step (extend vs. start new) is always correct because intervals are processed in start order.

class Solution {
    public int[][] merge(int[][] intervals) {
        Arrays.sort(intervals, (a, b) -> Integer.compare(a[0], b[0]));
        List<int[]> merged = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int[] iv : intervals) {
            if (merged.isEmpty() || iv[0] > merged.get(merged.size() - 1)[1]) {
                merged.add(iv);
            } else {
                int[] last = merged.get(merged.size() - 1);
                last[1] = Math.max(last[1], iv[1]);
            }
        }
        return merged.toArray(new int[merged.size()][]);
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    vector<vector<int>> merge(vector<vector<int>>& intervals) {
        sort(intervals.begin(), intervals.end(), [](const vector<int>& a, const vector<int>& b) {
            return a[0] < b[0];
        });
        vector<vector<int>> merged;
        for (auto& iv : intervals) {
            if (merged.empty() || iv[0] > merged.back()[1]) {
                merged.push_back(iv);
            } else {
                merged.back()[1] = max(merged.back()[1], iv[1]);
            }
        }
        return merged;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def merge(self, intervals: List[List[int]]) -> List[List[int]]:
        intervals.sort(key=lambda a: a[0])
        merged = []
        for iv in intervals:
            if not merged or iv[0] > merged[-1][1]:
                merged.append(iv)
            else:
                merged[-1][1] = max(merged[-1][1], iv[1])
        return merged
class Solution {
    merge(intervals: number[][]): number[][] {
        intervals.sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
        const merged: number[][] = [];
        for (const iv of intervals) {
            if (merged.length === 0 || iv[0] > merged[merged.length - 1][1]) {
                merged.push(iv);
            } else {
                merged[merged.length - 1][1] = Math.max(merged[merged.length - 1][1], iv[1]);
            }
        }
        return merged;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n log n) Space O(n) — sorting dominates; the sweep itself is linear.

Walkthrough. intervals = [[1,3],[2,6],[8,10],[15,18]] (already sorted by start):

stepcurrent intervalactionmerged list
1[1,3]list empty → add[[1,3]]
2[2,6]2 ≤ 3 → extend last end to 6[[1,6]]
3[8,10]8 > 6 → add new group[[1,6],[8,10]]
4[15,18]15 > 10 → add new group[[1,6],[8,10],[15,18]]

Result: [[1,6],[8,10],[15,18]].

17 · Math & Geometry

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern

Worked example — Spiral Matrix (LC 54, Medium)

Frequently asked at: Amazon, Microsoft, Google, Bloomberg.

Problem. Given an m x n matrix, return all elements of the matrix in spiral order (clockwise, starting top-left).

Idea. Maintain four shrinking boundaries: top, bottom, left, right. Walk right across the top row, down the right column, left across the bottom row, and up the left column, tightening each boundary after its pass. Guard the last two passes with boundary checks so a single remaining row or column isn't traversed twice.

class Solution {
    public List<Integer> spiralOrder(int[][] matrix) {
        List<Integer> result = new ArrayList<>();
        int top = 0, bottom = matrix.length - 1;
        int left = 0, right = matrix[0].length - 1;
        while (top <= bottom && left <= right) {
            for (int j = left; j <= right; j++) result.add(matrix[top][j]);
            top++;
            for (int i = top; i <= bottom; i++) result.add(matrix[i][right]);
            right--;
            if (top <= bottom) {
                for (int j = right; j >= left; j--) result.add(matrix[bottom][j]);
                bottom--;
            }
            if (left <= right) {
                for (int i = bottom; i >= top; i--) result.add(matrix[i][left]);
                left++;
            }
        }
        return result;
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    vector<int> spiralOrder(vector<vector<int>>& matrix) {
        vector<int> result;
        int top = 0, bottom = matrix.size() - 1;
        int left = 0, right = matrix[0].size() - 1;
        while (top <= bottom && left <= right) {
            for (int j = left; j <= right; j++) result.push_back(matrix[top][j]);
            top++;
            for (int i = top; i <= bottom; i++) result.push_back(matrix[i][right]);
            right--;
            if (top <= bottom) {
                for (int j = right; j >= left; j--) result.push_back(matrix[bottom][j]);
                bottom--;
            }
            if (left <= right) {
                for (int i = bottom; i >= top; i--) result.push_back(matrix[i][left]);
                left++;
            }
        }
        return result;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def spiralOrder(self, matrix: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        result = []
        top, bottom = 0, len(matrix) - 1
        left, right = 0, len(matrix[0]) - 1
        while top <= bottom and left <= right:
            for j in range(left, right + 1):
                result.append(matrix[top][j])
            top += 1
            for i in range(top, bottom + 1):
                result.append(matrix[i][right])
            right -= 1
            if top <= bottom:
                for j in range(right, left - 1, -1):
                    result.append(matrix[bottom][j])
                bottom -= 1
            if left <= right:
                for i in range(bottom, top - 1, -1):
                    result.append(matrix[i][left])
                left += 1
        return result
class Solution {
    spiralOrder(matrix: number[][]): number[] {
        const result: number[] = [];
        let top = 0, bottom = matrix.length - 1;
        let left = 0, right = matrix[0].length - 1;
        while (top <= bottom && left <= right) {
            for (let j = left; j <= right; j++) result.push(matrix[top][j]);
            top++;
            for (let i = top; i <= bottom; i++) result.push(matrix[i][right]);
            right--;
            if (top <= bottom) {
                for (let j = right; j >= left; j--) result.push(matrix[bottom][j]);
                bottom--;
            }
            if (left <= right) {
                for (let i = bottom; i >= top; i--) result.push(matrix[i][left]);
                left++;
            }
        }
        return result;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(m × n) Space O(1) extra — every cell visited exactly once, output list aside.

Walkthrough. matrix = [[1,2,3],[4,5,6],[7,8,9]], boundaries start at top=0, bottom=2, left=0, right=2:

passdirectionemittedboundaries after
1right along top row1, 2, 3top=1
2down right column6, 9right=1
3left along bottom row8, 7bottom=1
4up left column4left=1
5right along top row5top=2, loop ends (top>bottom)

Result: [1, 2, 3, 6, 9, 8, 7, 4, 5].

18 · Bit Manipulation

#ProblemDiffCompaniesSub-pattern

Worked example — Counting Bits (LC 338, Easy)

Frequently asked at: Google, Amazon, Adobe.

Problem. Given an integer n, return an array ans of length n + 1 where ans[i] is the number of 1 bits (popcount) in the binary representation of i, for every i from 0 to n.

Idea. Every integer i > 0 is i >> 1 shifted left by one bit, plus possibly a trailing 1. So its popcount equals the popcount of i >> 1 (already computed) plus the last bit i & 1: dp[i] = dp[i >> 1] + (i & 1). This turns an O(n log n) "count bits per number" scan into O(n) total work.

class Solution {
    public int[] countBits(int n) {
        int[] dp = new int[n + 1];
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            dp[i] = dp[i >> 1] + (i & 1);
        }
        return dp;
    }
}

class Solution {
public:
    vector<int> countBits(int n) {
        vector<int> dp(n + 1, 0);
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            dp[i] = dp[i >> 1] + (i & 1);
        }
        return dp;
    }
};
class Solution:
    def countBits(self, n: int) -> List[int]:
        dp = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            dp[i] = dp[i >> 1] + (i & 1)
        return dp
class Solution {
    countBits(n: number): number[] {
        const dp = new Array(n + 1).fill(0);
        for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
            dp[i] = dp[i >> 1] + (i & 1);
        }
        return dp;
    }
}

Complexity. Time O(n) Space O(n) — O(1) work per index, reusing the previously computed entry.

Walkthrough. n = 5:

i012345
i >> 1-01122
i & 1-10101
dp[i]011212

E.g. dp[3] = dp[1] + 1 = 1 + 1 = 2 (binary 11), and dp[5] = dp[2] + 1 = 1 + 1 = 2 (binary 101). Result: [0, 1, 1, 2, 1, 2].
